          Esta página contiene un mensaje de error/advertencia que podría revelar información sensible como la ubicación del archivo que produjo la excepción no controlada. Esta información puede ser usada para lanzas futuros ataques contra la aplicación web. La alerta podría ser una falso positivo si el mensaje de error es encontrado dentro de una página de documentación.

kemPtr;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Generate a new keypair for ML-KEM-768\n   *\n   * Generates a public/private keypair using the algorithm's internal\n   * random number generator. The secret key must be kept confidential.\n   *\n   * @returns {{publicKey: Uint8Array, secretKey: Uint8Array}}\n   * @throws {LibOQSOperationError} If keypair generation fails\n   * @throws {LibOQSError} If instance has been destroyed\n   * @example\n   * const { publicKey, secretKey } = kem.generateKeyPair();\n   * // publicKey: 1184 bytes\n   * // secretKey: 2400 bytes (keep confidential!)\n   */\n  generateKeyPair() {\n    this.#checkDestroyed();\n\n    const publicKeyPtr = this.#wasmModule._malloc(ML_KEM_768_INFO.keySize.publicKey);\n    const secretKeyPtr = this.#wasmModule._malloc(ML_KEM_768_INFO.keySize.secretKey);\n\n    try {\n      const result = this.#wasmModule._OQS_KEM_keypair(this.#kemPtr, publicKeyPtr, secretKeyPtr);\n\n      if (result !== 0) {\n        throw new LibOQSOperationError('keypair', 'ML-KEM-768', `Error code: ${result}`);\n      }\n\n      const publicKey = new Uint8Array(ML_KEM_768_INFO.keySize.publicKey);\n      const secretKey = new Uint8Array(ML_KEM_768_INFO.keySize.secretKey);\n\n      publicKey.set(this.#wasmModule.HEAPU8.subarray(publicKeyPtr, publicKeyPtr + ML_KEM_768_INFO.keySize.publicKey));\n      secretKey.set(this.#wasmModule.HEAPU8.subarray(secretKeyPtr, secretKeyPtr + ML_KEM_768_INFO.keySize.secretKey));\n\n      return { publicKey, secretKey };\n\n    } finally {\n      this.#wasmModule._free(publicKeyPtr);\n      this.#wasmModule._free(secretKeyPtr);\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Encapsulate a shared secret using a public key\n   *\n   * Generates a random shared secret and encapsulates it using the\n   * provided public key. {string}\n */\nexport const VERSION = '0.15.1';\n", "/**\n * Wire protocol shared by the server and the browser client.\n *\n * Both ends of the connection import this module, so the envelope definitions\n * and the validation limits exist exactly once in the codebase. A limit that\n * were declared separately on each side could drift apart silently, leaving a\n * client that accepts input the server will reject, or worse, a client that\n * refuses to send what the server would have accepted.\n *\n * Nothing in this file describes message *content*. From day 7 the `text`\n * field carries ciphertext and the server has no way to interpret it.\n */\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Validation limits\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/** Usernames are routing identifiers, so the character set is deliberately narrow. */\nexport const USERNAME_PATTERN = /^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{3,20}$/;\n\n/** Maximum length of a single message payload, in UTF-16 code units. */\nexport const MAX_TEXT_LENGTH = 4096;\n\n/**\n * Maximum length of the `text` field on the wire.\n *\n * Larger than MAX_TEXT_LENGTH because the field carries a base64-encoded\n * nonce, ciphertext and authentication tag rather than the message itself.\n * The plaintext bound is enforced by the client, which is the only party that\n * can see a plaintext; the server bounds only what it actually receives.\n */\nexport const MAX_PAYLOAD_LENGTH = 16 * 1024;\n\n/** Maximum size of a single WebSocket frame, in bytes. Enforced by `ws`. */\nexport const MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES = 64 * 1024;\n\n/** Standard base64 with optional padding. Key material travels as base64 text. */\nexport const BASE64_PATTERN = /^[A-Za-z0-9+/]+={0,2}$/;\n\n/** Generous upper bound for any single encoded public key or signature. */\nexport const MAX_KEY_LENGTH = 512;\n\n/** Size of a published one-time prekey batch. */\nexport const MAX_ONE_TIME_PREKEYS = 100;\n\n/** Per-recipient cap on messages held for an offline user. */\nexport const MAX_PENDING_PER_RECIPIENT = 200;\n\n/** Maximum accepted body size for the key publication endpoint. */\nexport const MAX_KEYS_BODY_BYTES = 128 * 1024;\n\n/** ML-KEM-768 keys are far larger than elliptic-curve ones: 1184 bytes encodes to about 1580 characters. */\nexport const MAX_KEM_KEY_LENGTH = 4096;\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Client -> server (WebSocket)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport interface RegisterMessage {\n  type: 'register';\n  username: string;\n}\n\nexport interface ChatMessage {\n  type: 'chat';\n  to: string;\n  /** Plaintext today; ciphertext from day 7 onwards. */\n  text: string;\n  /** X3DH / ratchet header, opaque to the server. Absent until day 6. */\n  header?: string;\n}\n\nexport type ClientMessage = RegisterMessage | ChatMessage;\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Server -> client (WebSocket)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport interface InfoMessage {\n  type: 'info';\n  text: string;\n}\n\nexport interface ErrorMessage {\n  type: 'error';\n  /** Machine-readable reason, e.g. `username_taken`, `rate_limit_exceeded`. */\n  code: string;\n  text: string;\n}\n\nexport interface IncomingChatMessage {\n  type: 'chat';\n  /** Set by the server from connection state, never copied from the sender's frame. */\n  from: string;\n  text: string;\n  header?: string;\n}\n\nexport type ServerMessage = InfoMessage | ErrorMessage | IncomingChatMessage;\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Prekey registry (HTTP)\n//\n// Every field below is public key material. Private keys never leave the\n// browser that generated them.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/** Body of `POST /keys`. All values base64-encoded. */\nexport interface PublishKeysRequest {\n  username: string;\n  identityKey: string;\n  signedPrekey: string;\n  /** Signature over the signed prekey, produced with the identity key. */\n  signedPrekeySignature: string;\n  oneTimePrekeys: string[];\n    /** ML-KEM-768 public key, or null from a client without post-quantum support. */\n  kemPrekey: string | null;\n  kemPrekeySignature: string | null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Response of `GET /keys/:username`.\n *\n * `oneTimePrekey` is null when the recipient's pool is exhausted. The sender\n * then performs the handshake with one fewer Diffie-Hellman, which is\n * permitted by X3DH at the cost of reduced forward secrecy for the first\n * message. `oneTimePrekeyId` tells the recipient which key to use when\n * reconstructing the shared secret.\n */\nexport interface PrekeyBundle {\n  username: string;\n  identityKey: string;\n  signedPrekey: string;\n  signedPrekeySignature: string;\n  oneTimePrekey: string | null;\n  oneTimePrekeyId: number | null;\n    /** ML-KEM-768 public key, or null from a client without post-quantum support. */\n  kemPrekey: string | null;\n  kemPrekeySignature: string | null;\n}\n\n/** True when `value` is a plausible base64 encoding of key material. */\nexport function isKeyMaterial(value: unknown): value is string {\n  return (\n    typeof value === 'string' &&\n    value.length > 0 &&\n    value.length <= MAX_KEY_LENGTH &&\n    BASE64_PATTERN.test(value)\n  );\n}\n\nexport interface PublishKeysResponse {\n  oneTimePrekeyIds: number[];\n  kemPrekey: string | null;\n}\n\nexport interface RegistryStatusResponse {\n  identityKey: string;\n  /** The published ML-KEM key, or null from a client without post-quantum support. */\n  kemPrekey: string | null;\n  availablePrekeyIds: number[];\n}\n\n/** True when `value` is a plausible base64 encoding of post-quantum key material. */\nexport function isKemKeyMaterial(value: unknown): value is string {\n  return (\n    typeof value === 'string' &&\n    value.length > 0 &&\n    value.length <= MAX_KEM_KEY_LENGTH &&\n    BASE64_PATTERN.test(value)\n  );\n}", "async function A(A={}){var I,g=A,C=!!globalThis.window,B=!!globalThis.WorkerGlobalScope,Q=(globalThis.process?.versions?.node&&globalThis.process,import.meta.url);if(C||B){try{new as to_hex,g as to_string,T as output_formats,m as base64_variants,r as libsodium};\n", "/**\n * libsodium bootstrap and encoding helpers for the browser client.\n *\n * The interim report recorded libsodium as unusable in the browser. The\n * obstacle was not the library but the loading method: it was being pulled\n * from a content delivery network, and the browser's tracking protection\n * blocked the request. Imported as a module and bundled into the application's\n * own script, the browser never makes an external request at all and the\n * problem disappears. The dependency was already present in package.json\n * throughout.\n *\n * All key material crossing the wire is encoded with the ORIGINAL base64\n * variant, matching the validation pattern in the shared protocol module.\n * libsodium defaults to URLSAFE_NO_PADDING, which would be silently rejected\n * by the server, so the variant is stated explicitly at every call site rather\n * than left to the default.\n */\n\nimport sodium from 'libsodium-wrappers';\n\nlet ready: Promise<void> | null = null;\n\n/**\n * Resolves once the WebAssembly module is initialised.\n *\n * Memoised, because `sodium.ready` must be awaited before any other call and\n * several independent parts of the client will need it. Calling any primitive\n * before this resolves yields undefined behaviour rather than an error, which\n * is a difficult failure to diagnose.\n */\nexport function initSodium(): Promise<void> {\n  if (ready === null) {\n    ready = sodium.ready;\n  }\n  return ready;\n}\n\nexport function toBase64(bytes: Uint8Array): string {\n  return sodium.to_base64(bytes, sodium.base64_variants.ORIGINAL);\n}\n\nexport function fromBase64(text: string): Uint8Array {\n  return sodium.from_base64(text, sodium.base64_variants.ORIGINAL);\n}\n\n/**\n * Verifies that X25519 Diffie-Hellman works end to end in this browser.\n *\n * Generating a keypair alone would only prove that the module loaded. The test\n * performs a full exchange between two locally generated keypairs and asserts\n * that both sides reach the same shared secret, which is the exact primitive\n * day 6 depends on. Comparison uses libsodium's constant-time `memcmp` rather\n * than a loop; the timing of this particular comparison is not sensitive, but\n * establishing the habit here avoids writing a variable-time comparison\n * somewhere it does matter.\n *\n * Returns the shared secret so the caller can display it as evidence.\n */\nexport async function verifyX25519Exchange(): Promise<Uint8Array> {\n  await initSodium();\n\n  // crypto_box keypairs are X25519 keypairs.\n  const alice = sodium.crypto_box_keypair();\n  const bob = sodium.crypto_box_keypair();\n\n  const aliceView = sodium.crypto_scalarmult(alice.privateKey, bob.publicKey);\n  const bobView = sodium.crypto_scalarmult(bob.privateKey, alice.publicKey);\n\n  if (!sodium.memcmp(aliceView, bobView)) {\n    throw new Error('X25519 smoke test failed: the two parties derived different secrets');\n  }\n\n  return aliceView;\n}\n\nexport { sodium };\n", "/**\n * Post-quantum key encapsulation: ML-KEM-768.\n *\n * WHY A KEM AND NOT A DIFFIE-HELLMAN\n *\n * X25519 is a key agreement: both parties contribute a public value and each\n * combines its own private half with the other's public half to reach the\n * same secret. ML-KEM is a key encapsulation mechanism, which works\n * differently and asymmetrically. One party publishes a public key; the other\n * generates a secret, encapsulates it under that key, and sends the resulting\n * ciphertext. The holder of the secret key decapsulates it and recovers the\n * same value. There is no operation corresponding to combining two public\n * keys, which is why the hybrid handshake cannot simply substitute one\n * primitive for the other and has to carry both.\n *\n * WHY ML-KEM-768\n *\n * It is the parameter set NIST describes as the general-purpose choice, at\n * security category 3. ML-KEM-512 would halve the key and ciphertext sizes\n * but sits at category 1, which is weaker than the X25519 half of the hybrid\n * already provides and would make the post-quantum component the limiting\n * factor. ML-KEM-1024 costs more bandwidth for protection beyond what this\n * prototype's threat model asks for.\n *\n * THE COST, WHICH IS THE POINT OF MEASURING IT\n *\n * An ML-KEM-768 public key is 1184 bytes and a ciphertext is 1088, against 32\n * bytes for an X25519 public key. The handshake header therefore grows by\n * roughly a factor of thirty-five. That is the central trade-off of the\n * post-quantum transition and the reason the benchmarks exist: the shared\n * secret is the same 32 bytes either way, so everything downstream of the\n * handshake is unchanged, and the cost is paid entirely in the exchange.\n *\n * WHY THE LIBRARY IS INJECTED RATHER THAN IMPORTED\n *\n * `@oqs/liboqs-js` publishes no CommonJS entry point, so a direct import\n * would compile to a `require` that cannot load it, and the module would be\n * usable from the bundled client but not from the test suite. Receiving the\n * factory from the caller keeps both working: the browser supplies it from\n * the application's entry point, and the tests supply it through a dynamic\n * import. The type is imported with `import type`, which the compiler erases,\n * so no runtime dependency is introduced by it.\n *\n * The result is that this module depends on the capability rather than on the\n * package, which is also what would allow the implementation to be swapped\n * for another without touching anything above it.\n *\n * MEMORY\n *\n * The implementation is WebAssembly, and WASM memory is not reclaimed by the\n * garbage collector. The instance is created once and kept for the lifetime\n * of the page rather than per operation, which avoids both the repeated\n * initialisation cost and the leak that creating one per handshake would\n * produce.\n */\n\nimport type { MLKEM768 } from '@oqs/liboqs-js';\n\n/** Sizes in bytes, from the algorithm's own metadata. Asserted on first use. */\nexport const ML_KEM_768_PUBLIC_KEY_BYTES = 1184;\nexport const ML_KEM_768_SECRET_KEY_BYTES = 2400;\nexport const ML_KEM_768_CIPHERTEXT_BYTES = 1088;\nexport const ML_KEM_768_SHARED_SECRET_BYTES = 32;\n\nexport interface KemKeyPair {\n  publicKey: Uint8Array;\n  secretKey: Uint8Array;\n}\n\nexport interface Encapsulation {\n  ciphertext: Uint8Array;\n  sharedSecret: Uint8Array;\n}\n\n/** Produces an initialised ML-KEM-768 instance. */\nexport type MlKemFactory = () => Promise<MLKEM768>;\n\nlet factory: MlKemFactory | null = null;\nlet instance: Promise<MLKEM768> | null = null;\n\n/**\n * Supplies the implementation. Called once, at the application's entry point\n * or at the start of a test run.\n */\nexport function useMlKemFactory(supplied: MlKemFactory): void {\n  factory = supplied;\n  instance = null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Resolves the shared instance, initialising it on first use.\n *\n * Memoised for the same reason `initSodium` is: the WebAssembly module must\n * be instantiated before any call, and several independent parts of the\n * client will need it.\n */\nexport function initMlKem(): Promise<MLKEM768> {\n  if (factory === null) {\n    throw new Error('No ML-KEM implementation supplied; call useMlKemFactory first');\n  }\n  if (instance === null) {\n    instance = factory();\n  }\n  return instance;\n}\n\n/**\n * Releases the WebAssembly instance.\n *\n * Only required where instances would otherwise accumulate, such as between\n * test runs; the browser client holds one for the lifetime of the page.\n */\nexport async function releaseMlKem(): Promise<void> {\n  if (instance === null) {\n    return;\n  }\n  const kem = await instance;\n  instance = null;\n  kem.destroy();\n}\n\nexport async function generateKemKeyPair(): Promise<KemKeyPair> {\n  const kem = await initMlKem();\n  return kem.generateKeyPair();\n}\n\n/**\n * Generates a shared secret and encapsulates it under the recipient's public\n * key. Performed by the initiating party.\n */\nexport async function encapsulate(publicKey: Uint8Array): Promise<Encapsulation> {\n  if (publicKey.length !== ML_KEM_768_PUBLIC_KEY_BYTES) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `Expected a ${ML_KEM_768_PUBLIC_KEY_BYTES}-byte ML-KEM public key, ` +\n        `received ${publicKey.length}`\n    );\n  }\n  const kem = await initMlKem();\n  return kem.encapsulate(publicKey);\n}\n\n/**\n * Recovers the shared secret from a ciphertext. Performed by the responding\n * party, which holds the secret key.\n */\nexport async function decapsulate(\n  ciphertext: Uint8Array,\n  secretKey: Uint8Array\n): Promise<Uint8Array> {\n  if (ciphertext.length !== ML_KEM_768_CIPHERTEXT_BYTES) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `Expected a ${ML_KEM_768_CIPHERTEXT_BYTES}-byte ML-KEM ciphertext, ` +\n        `received ${ciphertext.length}`\n    );\n  }\n  const kem = await initMlKem();\n  return kem.decapsulate(ciphertext, secretKey);\n}\n\n/**\n * Verifies that encapsulation works end to end in this environment.\n *\n * Generating a keypair alone would establish only that the module loaded.\n * What the hybrid handshake depends on is that the encapsulating and\n * decapsulating parties reach the same secret, so the test performs a\n * complete round trip and compares the results. It also checks the sizes\n * against the constants above, which the header parsing relies on.\n *\n * Returns the shared secret and the time taken, so the caller can report both.\n */\nexport async function verifyMlKemExchange(): Promise<{\n  sharedSecret: Uint8Array;\n  elapsedMs: number;\n}> {\n  const kem = await initMlKem();\n\n  const started = performance.now();\n  const { publicKey, secretKey } = kem.generateKeyPair();\n  const { ciphertext, sharedSecret } = kem.encapsulate(publicKey);\n  const recovered = kem.decapsulate(ciphertext, secretKey);\n  const elapsedMs = performance.now() - started;\n\n  if (\n    publicKey.length !== ML_KEM_768_PUBLIC_KEY_BYTES ||\n    secretKey.length !== ML_KEM_768_SECRET_KEY_BYTES ||\n    ciphertext.length !== ML_KEM_768_CIPHERTEXT_BYTES ||\n    sharedSecret.length !== ML_KEM_768_SHARED_SECRET_BYTES\n  ) {\n    throw new Error('ML-KEM-768 produced values of unexpected length');\n  }\n\n  if (\n    recovered.length !== sharedSecret.length ||\n    !sharedSecret.every((byte, index) => byte === recovered[index])\n  ) {\n    throw new Error('ML-KEM smoke test failed: the two parties derived different secrets');\n  }\n\n  return { sharedSecret, elapsedMs };\n}\n", "/**\n * Long-term key material: generation, local persistence, publication and\n * reconciliation with the registry.\n *\n * X3DH requires the identity key to do two things that no single curve\n * provides: sign the prekey, and participate in Diffie-Hellman. Signal solves\n * this with XEdDSA, which produces Ed25519-style signatures directly from an\n * X25519 key. libsodium does not expose XEdDSA, so this implementation takes\n * the standard alternative: the identity key is an Ed25519 keypair, and both\n * halves are converted to X25519 when a Diffie-Hellman is required. The two\n * representations are the same underlying key, so this preserves the property\n * X3DH depends on, that the key used for the authenticating DH is the key that\n * signed the prekey.\n *\n * The identity key also signs the post-quantum prekey, for the same reason it\n * signs the classical one: without a signature the relay could substitute its\n * own encapsulation key and recover the post-quantum half of every handshake\n * it served.\n *\n * PRIVATE KEYS ARE STORED IN CLEARTEXT IN IndexedDB. This is a structural\n * limitation of browser-based end-to-end encryption, not an oversight. The\n * Web Crypto API can hold non-extractable keys that script cannot read, but\n * libsodium operates on raw key bytes and therefore cannot use them; the two\n * are mutually exclusive. The consequence is that script execution in this\n * origin is equivalent to full compromise of the user's conversations, which\n * is why the cross-site scripting vector in the renderer was removed before\n * any key material existed to steal.\n *\n * Material is stored per username. Two tabs of the same browser share one\n * IndexedDB, so a single global record would give every local user the same\n * identity and make a handshake between them meaningless.\n */\n\nimport {\n  MAX_ONE_TIME_PREKEYS,\n  type PrekeyBundle,\n  type PublishKeysRequest,\n  type PublishKeysResponse,\n  type RegistryStatusResponse,\n} from '../shared/protocol';\nimport { initSodium, sodium, toBase64, fromBase64 } from './crypto';\nimport { generateKemKeyPair, initMlKem, type KemKeyPair } from './pqc';\n\nconst DB_NAME = 'burblit';\nconst DB_VERSION = 1;\nconst STORE_NAME = 'identities';\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Types\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport interface KeyPair {\n  publicKey: Uint8Array;\n  privateKey: Uint8Array;\n}\n\nexport interface Identity {\n  username: string;\n\n  /** Ed25519. Published; converted to X25519 for Diffie-Hellman. */\n  identityKeyPair: KeyPair;\n\n  /** X25519. Rotated periodically in a production system; fixed here. */\n  signedPrekeyPair: KeyPair;\n\n  /** Detached Ed25519 signature over the signed prekey's public key. */\n  signedPrekeySignature: Uint8Array;\n\n  /**\n   * One-time prekeys, indexed by the identifier the server assigned at\n   * publication. The sender names an identifier in the handshake header, and\n   * this map is what lets the recipient find the matching private key.\n   */\n  oneTimePrekeys: Record<number, KeyPair>;\n\n  /**\n   * ML-KEM-768 keypair, used for the post-quantum half of a hybrid handshake.\n   *\n   * Optional because a record written before post-quantum support existed has\n   * none. Such a record is treated as diverged and republished rather than\n   * read as a client that has opted out.\n   */\n  kemKeyPair?: KemKeyPair;\n\n  /** Detached Ed25519 signature over the ML-KEM public key. */\n  kemPrekeySignature?: Uint8Array;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// IndexedDB access\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nfunction openDatabase(): Promise<IDBDatabase> {\n  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n    const request = indexedDB.open(DB_NAME, DB_VERSION);\n    request.onupgradeneeded = () => {\n      const database = request.result;\n      if (!database.objectStoreNames.contains(STORE_NAME)) {\n        database.createObjectStore(STORE_NAME, { keyPath: 'username' });\n      }\n    };\n    request.onsuccess = () => resolve(request.result);\n    request.onerror = () => reject(request.error);\n  });\n}\n\nfunction readIdentity(username: string): Promise<Identity | null> {\n  return openDatabase().then(\n    (database) =>\n      new Promise<Identity | null>((resolve, reject) => {\n        const transaction = database.transaction(STORE_NAME, 'readonly');\n        const request = transaction.objectStore(STORE_NAME).get(username);\n        request.onsuccess = () => resolve((request.result as Identity | undefined) ?? null);\n        request.onerror = () => reject(request.error);\n        transaction.oncomplete = () => database.close();\n      })\n  );\n}\n\nfunction writeIdentity(identity: Identity): Promise<void> {\n  return openDatabase().then(\n    (database) =>\n      new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {\n        const transaction = database.transaction(STORE_NAME, 'readwrite');\n        transaction.objectStore(STORE_NAME).put(identity);\n        transaction.oncomplete = () => {\n          database.close();\n          resolve();\n        };\n        transaction.onerror = () => reject(transaction.error);\n      })\n  );\n}\n\n/** Discards stored material for a user, forcing regeneration on next connect. */\nexport function clearIdentity(username: string): Promise<void> {\n  return openDatabase().then(\n    (database) =>\n      new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {\n        const transaction = database.transaction(STORE_NAME, 'readwrite');\n        transaction.objectStore(STORE_NAME).delete(username);\n        transaction.oncomplete = () => {\n          database.close();\n          resolve();\n        };\n        transaction.onerror = () => reject(transaction.error);\n      })\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Curve conversion\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport function identityPublicToX25519(ed25519PublicKey: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {\n  return sodium.crypto_sign_ed25519_pk_to_curve25519(ed25519PublicKey);\n}\n\nexport function identityPrivateToX25519(ed25519PrivateKey: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {\n  return sodium.crypto_sign_ed25519_sk_to_curve25519(ed25519PrivateKey);\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Generation\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport function generateOneTimePrekeys(): KeyPair[] {\n  const pairs: KeyPair[] = [];\n  for (let i = 0; i < MAX_ONE_TIME_PREKEYS; i += 1) {\n    pairs.push(sodium.crypto_box_keypair());\n  }\n  return pairs;\n}\n\nexport function generateLongTermMaterial(): {\n  identityKeyPair: KeyPair;\n  signedPrekeyPair: KeyPair;\n  signedPrekeySignature: Uint8Array;\n} {\n  const signing = sodium.crypto_sign_keypair();\n  const identityKeyPair: KeyPair = {\n    publicKey: signing.publicKey,\n    privateKey: signing.privateKey,\n  };\n\n  const signedPrekeyPair = sodium.crypto_box_keypair();\n\n  // The signature is what prevents the relay from substituting its own prekey\n  // into a bundle it serves. A client that verifies it will not complete a\n  // handshake against key material the claimed identity did not vouch for.\n  const signedPrekeySignature = sodium.crypto_sign_detached(\n    signedPrekeyPair.publicKey,\n    identityKeyPair.privateKey\n  );\n\n  return { identityKeyPair, signedPrekeyPair, signedPrekeySignature };\n}\n\n/**\n * Generates the post-quantum prekey and signs it with the identity key.\n *\n * Kept separate from the classical material because it is asynchronous: the\n * WebAssembly module must be instantiated before a keypair can be produced.\n */\nexport async function generateKemMaterial(identityPrivateKey: Uint8Array): Promise<{\n  kemKeyPair: KemKeyPair;\n  kemPrekeySignature: Uint8Array;\n}> {\n  await initMlKem();\n  const kemKeyPair = await ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Registry interaction\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nasync function registryStatus(username: string): Promise<RegistryStatusResponse | null> {\n  const response = await fetch(`/keys/${encodeURIComponent(username)}/status`);\n  if (response.status === 404) {\n    return null;\n  }\n  if (!response.ok) {\n    throw new Error(`Registry status check failed with status ${response.status}`);\n  }\n  return (await response.json()) as RegistryStatusResponse;\n}\n\nasync function publish(identity: Identity, oneTimePrekeyPairs: KeyPair[]): Promise<number[]> {\n  const body: PublishKeysRequest = {\n    username: identity.username,\n    identityKey: toBase64(identity.identityKeyPair.publicKey),\n    signedPrekey: toBase64(identity.signedPrekeyPair.publicKey),\n    signedPrekeySignature: toBase64(identity.signedPrekeySignature),\n    oneTimePrekeys: oneTimePrekeyPairs.map((pair) => toBase64(pair.publicKey)),\n    kemPrekey: identity.kemKeyPair === undefined ? null : toBase64(identity.kemKeyPair.publicKey),\n    kemPrekeySignature:\n      identity.kemPrekeySignature === undefined ? null : toBase64(identity.kemPrekeySignature),\n  };\n\n  const response = await fetch('/keys', {\n    method: 'POST',\n    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n    body: JSON.stringify(body),\n  });\n\n  if (!response.ok) {\n    throw new Error(`Key publication rejected with status ${response.status}`);\n  }\n\n  const { oneTimePrekeyIds } = (await response.json()) as PublishKeysResponse;\n\n  if (oneTimePrekeyIds.length !== oneTimePrekeyPairs.length) {\n    throw new Error('Server returned an unexpected number of prekey identifiers');\n  }\n\n  return oneTimePrekeyIds;\n}\n\nfunction indexById(ids: number[], pairs: KeyPair[]): Record<number, KeyPair> {\n  const indexed: Record<number, KeyPair> = {};\n  ids.forEach((id, index) => {\n    indexed[id] = pairs[index];\n  });\n  return indexed;\n}\n\n/**\n * True when the registry's record of this user is one the stored material can\n * actually service.\n *\n * All three conditions are necessary. The identity key must match, or a\n * correspondent would authenticate against a key this client cannot use.\n * Every prekey the registry is still willing to serve must be held locally,\n * because a republication replaces the batch without touching the identity\n * key. And the published post-quantum key must be the one this client holds\n * the secret half of, or a correspondent would encapsulate to a key that\n * cannot be decapsulated and the hybrid handshake would fail at the far end\n * with nothing to indicate why.\n */\nfunction canService(stored: Identity, status: RegistryStatusResponse | null): boolean {\n  if (status === null) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  if (status.identityKey !== toBase64(stored.identityKeyPair.publicKey)) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  if (stored.kemKeyPair === undefined || stored.kemPrekeySignature === undefined) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  if (status.kemPrekey !== toBase64(stored.kemKeyPair.publicKey)) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  return status.availablePrekeyIds.every((id) => stored.oneTimePrekeys[id] !== undefined);\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Entry point\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * Serialises identity loading per username.\n *\n * Publication is destructive: the server replaces a user's entire prekey batch\n * on each call. Two concurrent loads therefore race, and the loser's batch is\n * deleted from the registry while its identifiers remain in local storage,\n * leaving the client unable to answer any handshake.\n */\nconst inFlight = new Map<string, Promise<Identity>>();\n\nexport function loadOrCreateIdentity(username: string): Promise<Identity> {\n  const existing = inFlight.get(username);\n  if (existing !== undefined) {\n    return existing;\n  }\n\n  const task = resolveIdentity(username);\n  inFlight.set(\n    username,\n    task.finally(() => {\n      inFlight.delete(username);\n    })\n  );\n  return task;\n}\n\n/**\n * Returns the user's identity, creating or re-registering it as required.\n *\n * Stored material is not trusted on its own. The client's IndexedDB and the\n * server's registry are two independent stores that can diverge: the server's\n * database may be reset, or a batch replaced. When they diverge the failure is\n * silent and unrecoverable, because a correspondent is served a prekey\n * identifier the recipient does not hold and every handshake between the two\n * aborts. Reconciling on each connection turns that permanent failure into a\n * transparent repair.\n *\n * Divergence is repaired by republishing the SAME long-term keys, rather than\n * by generating a new identity. The fingerprint a correspondent may have\n * verified out of band therefore survives a server-side reset, which a full\n * regeneration would silently invalidate.\n */\nasync function resolveIdentity(username: string): Promise<Identity> {\n  await initSodium();\n\n  const stored = await readIdentity(username);\n\n  if (stored !== null) {\n    if (canService(stored, await registryStatus(username))) {\n      return stored;\n    }\n\n    console.warn(`Registry diverged from stored material for ${username}; republishing`);\n\n    // A record written before post-quantum support existed carries no KEM\n    // keypair, so one is generated and signed by the existing identity key.\n    // The identity itself is preserved.\n    const repaired: Identity = { ...stored };\n    if (repaired.kemKeyPair === undefined || repaired.kemPrekeySignature === undefined) {\n      const kem = await generateKemMaterial(stored.identityKeyPair.privateKey);\n      repaired.kemKeyPair = kem.kemKeyPair;\n      repaired.kemPrekeySignature = kem.kemPrekeySignature;\n    }\n\n    const oneTimePrekeyPairs = generateOneTimePrekeys();\n    const ids = await publish(repaired, oneTimePrekeyPairs);\n    repaired.oneTimePrekeys = indexById(ids, oneTimePrekeyPairs);\n\n    await writeIdentity(repaired);\n    return repaired;\n  }\n\n  const { identityKeyPair, signedPrekeyPair, signedPrekeySignature } = generateLongTermMaterial();\n  const { kemKeyPair, kemPrekeySignature } = await generateKemMaterial(\n    identityKeyPair.privateKey\n  );\n  const oneTimePrekeyPairs = generateOneTimePrekeys();\n\n  const identity: Identity = {\n    username,\n    identityKeyPair,\n    signedPrekeyPair,\n    signedPrekeySignature,\n    oneTimePrekeys: {},\n    kemKeyPair,\n    kemPrekeySignature,\n  };\n\n  const ids = await publish(identity, oneTimePrekeyPairs);\n  identity.oneTimePrekeys = indexById(ids, oneTimePrekeyPairs);\n\n  // Stored only after a successful publication. Persisting first would leave\n  // a client holding private keys whose public halves the registry never\n  // received, and which no correspondent could ever use.\n  await writeIdentity(identity);\n  return identity;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Verification\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * Checks that a fetched bundle's prekeys were vouched for by the identity key\n * published alongside them.\n *\n * This is the check that constrains a malicious relay. It does not establish\n * that the identity key belongs to the named user, which the registry cannot\n * prove: it establishes only that the bundle is internally consistent, so the\n * server cannot splice its own prekey into someone else's bundle and read the\n * conversation. Binding a name to a key requires an out-of-band channel and\n * remains future work.\n *\n * The post-quantum prekey is verified on the same terms when present. A\n * bundle that carries one without a signature is rejected rather than treated\n * as classical-only, since accepting it would let a relay strip the signature\n * and supply its own encapsulation key.\n */\nexport async function verifyBundle(bundle: PrekeyBundle): Promise<boolean> {\n  await initSodium();\n  try {\n    const signedPrekeyValid = sodium.crypto_sign_verify_detached(\n      fromBase64(bundle.signedPrekeySignature),\n      fromBase64(bundle.signedPrekey),\n      fromBase64(bundle.identityKey)\n    );\n\n    if (!signedPrekeyValid) {\n      return false;\n    }\n\n    if (bundle.kemPrekey === null) {\n      return true;\n    }\n\n    if (bundle.kemPrekeySignature === null) {\n      return false;\n    }\n\n    return sodium.crypto_sign_verify_detached(\n      fromBase64(bundle.kemPrekeySignature),\n      fromBase64(bundle.kemPrekey),\n      fromBase64(bundle.identityKey)\n    );\n  } catch {\n    // Malformed base64 or a wrong-length key reaches here as an exception.\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n", "/**\n * The key agreement abstraction.\n *\n * Two features of the remaining scope, the Double Ratchet and the hybrid\n * post-quantum handshake, both act on the same point of the system: the root\n * key from which every subsequent message key descends. Written as patches\n * over existing code they are two rewrites of the same region. Written behind\n * a common interface they are two interchangeable implementations, and the\n * ratchet can consume a root key without knowing, or caring, which agreement\n * produced it.\n *\n * `X25519Agreement` (day 6) performs a simplified X3DH. `HybridAgreement`\n * (day 14) combines the same X25519 exchange with an ML-KEM encapsulation and\n * derives the root key from both, so that the result remains secure as long as\n * at least one of the two underlying problems is hard. Because both satisfy\n * this interface, the same conversation can be run in either mode and the\n * difference measured, which is what turns Objective 4 from an estimate into\n * a measurement.\n *\n * If the abstraction leaks anywhere, it will show up on day 15 when the mode\n * is switched at run time. That day exists partly to detect it.\n */\n\nimport type { PrekeyBundle } from './protocol';\n\n/** Length of the root key, in bytes. Fixed by the ratchet, not by the agreement. */\nexport const ROOT_KEY_BYTES = 32;\n\n/**\n * Which agreement produced a root key.\n *\n * Carried so that day 16 can label measurements and day 15 can negotiate the\n * mode without the ratchet or the transport needing to understand either\n * construction.\n */\nexport type AgreementMode = 'x25519' | 'hybrid';\n\n/**\n * The initiator's output.\n *\n * `header` is the opaque material the responder needs in order to reach the\n * same root key: ephemeral public keys, the identifier of the one-time prekey\n * consumed, and for the hybrid mode the ML-KEM ciphertext. It travels with the\n * first message and is meaningless to the server.\n */\nexport interface InitiationResult {\n  rootKey: Uint8Array;\n  header: Uint8Array;\n}\n\nexport interface ResponseResult {\n  rootKey: Uint8Array;\n}\n\nexport interface KeyAgreement {\n  /** Identifies the construction, for negotiation and for benchmarking. */\n  readonly mode: AgreementMode;\n\n  /**\n   * Derives a root key as the initiating party, from the recipient's\n   * published bundle. Returns the header that lets the recipient derive the\n   * same value.\n   */\n  initiate(peerBundle: PrekeyBundle): Promise<InitiationResult>;\n\n  /** Derives the same root key as the responding party, from the header. */\n  respond(header: Uint8Array): Promise<ResponseResult>;\n}\n", "/**\n * A hybrid classical and post-quantum key agreement.\n *\n * WHAT HYBRID MEANS, AND WHY\n *\n * The root key is derived from both an X3DH exchange and an ML-KEM\n * encapsulation, concatenated into a single derivation. The resulting secret\n * is safe as long as at least one of the two underlying problems is hard, so\n * the construction can only improve on either component alone: an adversary\n * must break both the elliptic-curve discrete logarithm and module lattices\n * to recover it.\n *\n * That asymmetry of risk is the entire argument for hybrids during a\n * transition. X25519 has two decades of analysis behind it and a known\n * weakness against a sufficiently large quantum computer. ML-KEM resists that\n * attack but is comparatively young, and the history of post-quantum\n * candidates includes schemes broken late in their evaluation, SIKE most\n * prominently. Deploying either alone means accepting one of those risks\n * whole; deploying both means accepting neither.\n *\n * THE THREAT THIS ADDRESSES\n *\n * The attack is not hypothetical and does not require a quantum computer to\n * exist today. An adversary can record encrypted traffic now and decrypt it\n * once the capability arrives, which is why messages whose confidentiality\n * must outlast the hardware need post-quantum protection at the time they are\n * sent rather than later.\n *\n * WHY THE TWO PRIMITIVES ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE\n *\n * X25519 is a key agreement: both parties publish a value and each combines\n * its private half with the other's public half. ML-KEM is a key\n * encapsulation mechanism, which is asymmetric: the recipient publishes a\n * public key, the sender generates a secret and encapsulates it, and the\n * recipient decapsulates. There is no operation that combines two ML-KEM\n * public keys, so the hybrid cannot substitute one for the other. It carries\n * both, and the extra traffic is almost entirely the KEM ciphertext.\n *\n * WHAT IMPLICIT REJECTION MEANS HERE\n *\n * ML-KEM does not report a corrupted ciphertext as an error. It performs\n * implicit rejection, returning a deterministic but unrelated value, which\n * denies an adversary the oracle a distinguishable failure would provide. The\n * handshake therefore cannot learn from decapsulation whether the exchange\n * was tampered with: the two parties simply derive different root keys, and\n * the discrepancy surfaces when the first message fails to authenticate.\n *\n * SIMPLIFICATION AGAINST THE SPECIFICATION\n *\n * Signal's PQXDH publishes one-time KEM prekeys alongside a signed one. Here\n * only the signed key is published, so the post-quantum component of the\n * initial message is not covered by forward secrecy of its own: an adversary\n * who later obtained the KEM secret key could recover the post-quantum half\n * of a recorded handshake. The classical half retains its forward secrecy,\n * and the ratchet supersedes both after the first exchange. This is recorded\n * in the critical reflection rather than presented as equivalent to the\n * specification.\n */\n\nimport {\n  ROOT_KEY_BYTES,\n  type AgreementMode,\n  type InitiationResult,\n  type KeyAgreement,\n  type ResponseResult,\n} from '../shared/keyAgreement';\nimport type { PrekeyBundle } from '../shared/protocol';\nimport { initSodium, sodium, toBase64, fromBase64 } from './crypto';\nimport {\n  identityPrivateToX25519,\n  identityPublicToX25519,\n  verifyBundle,\n  type Identity,\n} from './identity';\nimport { decapsulate, encapsulate, initMlKem, ML_KEM_768_CIPHERTEXT_BYTES } from './pqc';\n\n/**\n * The hybrid handshake header.\n *\n * Identical to the classical header except for `ct`, the ML-KEM ciphertext,\n * which is 1088 bytes and dominates the size of the whole structure. Nothing\n * in it is secret: a ciphertext is meaningless without the secret key that\n * decapsulates it.\n */\nexport interface HybridHandshakeHeader {\n  v: 1;\n  mode: 'hybrid';\n  /** Initiator's Ed25519 identity key, base64. */\n  ik: string;\n  /** Initiator's ephemeral X25519 public key, base64. */\n  ek: string;\n  /** Identifier of the consumed one-time prekey, or null if none was served. */\n  opk: number | null;\n  /** ML-KEM-768 ciphertext encapsulating the post-quantum secret, base64. */\n  ct: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Domain separator. Distinct from the classical one, so the two constructions\n * cannot produce the same root key from related inputs.\n */\nconst DERIVATION_CONTEXT = 'Burblit-PQXDH-v1-root';\n\n/**\n * Derives the root key from the concatenated exchange outputs.\n *\n * The post-quantum secret is appended to the Diffie-Hellman outputs and the\n * whole sequence hashed once, which is what makes the result depend on every\n * component: an adversary who recovers all four Diffie-Hellman values but not\n * the encapsulated secret learns nothing, and the converse holds as well.\n */\nfunction deriveRootKey(contributions: Uint8Array[], associatedData: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {\n  const totalLength = contributions.reduce((sum, part) => sum + part.length, 0);\n  const concatenated = new Uint8Array(totalLength);\n\n  let offset = 0;\n  for (const part of contributions) {\n    concatenated.set(part, offset);\n    offset += part.length;\n  }\n\n  const derivationKey = sodium.crypto_generichash(\n    sodium.crypto_generichash_KEYBYTES,\n    associatedData,\n    sodium.from_string(DERIVATION_CONTEXT)\n  );\n\n  return sodium.crypto_generichash(ROOT_KEY_BYTES, concatenated, derivationKey);\n}\n\n/** Concatenates both identity keys in a fixed order, initiator first. */\nfunction associatedData(initiatorIdentity: Uint8Array, responderIdentity: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {\n  const combined = new Uint8Array(initiatorIdentity.length + responderIdentity.length);\n  combined.set(initiatorIdentity, 0);\n  combined.set(responderIdentity, initiatorIdentity.length);\n  return combined;\n}\n\n/** True when a correspondent has published the material a hybrid handshake needs. */\nexport function supportsHybrid(bundle: PrekeyBundle): boolean {\n  return bundle.kemPrekey !== null && bundle.kemPrekeySignature !== null;\n}\n\nexport class HybridAgreement implements KeyAgreement {\n  readonly mode: AgreementMode = 'hybrid';\n\n  constructor(private readonly identity: Identity) {}\n\n  async initiate(peerBundle: PrekeyBundle): Promise<InitiationResult> {\n    await initSodium();\n    await initMlKem();\n\n    if (!(await verifyBundle(peerBundle))) {\n      throw new Error(\n        `Bundle for ${peerBundle.username} failed signature verification; refusing to proceed`\n      );\n    }\n\n    if (peerBundle.kemPrekey === null) {\n      throw new Error(\n        `${peerBundle.username} has published no post-quantum key; a hybrid handshake ` +\n          'is not available with this correspondent'\n      );\n fromBase64(peerBundle.signedPrekey);\n\n    // Ephemeral keypair. Its private half is never stored and goes out of\n    // scope when this function returns, which is what the classical half of\n    // the forward secrecy rests on.\n    const ephemeral = sodium.crypto_box_keypair();\n    const ownIdentityX = identityPrivateToX25519(this.identity.identityKeyPair.privateKey);\n\n    const contributions: Uint8Array[] = [\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownIdentityX, peerSignedPrekey),\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ephemeral.privateKey, peerIdentityX),\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ephemeral.privateKey, peerSignedPrekey),\n    ];\n\n    if (peerBundle.oneTimePrekey !== null) {\n      contributions.push(\n        sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ephemeral.privateKey, fromBase64(peerBundle.oneTimePrekey))\n      );\n    }\n\n    // The post-quantum contribution. The secret is generated here and never\n    // transmitted; only the ciphertext travels, and it is useless without the\n    // recipient's secret key.\n    const { ciphertext, sharedSecret } = await encapsulate(fromBase64(peerBundle.kemPrekey));\n    contributions.push(sharedSecret);\n\n    const rootKey = deriveRootKey(\n      contributions,\n      associatedData(this.identity.identityKeyPair.publicKey, peerIdentityEd)\n    );\n\n    const header: HybridHandshakeHeader = {\n      v: 1,\n      mode: 'hybrid',\n      ik: toBase64(this.identity.identityKeyPair.publicKey),\n      ek: toBase64(ephemeral.publicKey),\n      opk: peerBundle.oneTimePrekeyId,\n      ct: toBase64(ciphertext),\n    };\n\n    return { rootKey, header: sodium.from_string(JSON.stringify(header)) };\n  }\n\n  async respond(header: Uint8Array): Promise<ResponseResult> {\n    await initSodium();\n    await initMlKem();\n\n    const parsed = parseHybridHeader(header);\n\n    if (this.identity.kemKeyPair === undefined) {\n      throw new Error('This client holds no post-quantum key and cannot answer a hybrid handshake');\n    }\n\n    const peerIdentityEd = fromBase64(parsed.ik);\n    const peerIdentityX = identityPublicToX25519(peerIdentityEd);\n    const peerEphemeral = fromBase64(parsed.ek);\n\n    const ownIdentityX = identityPrivateToX25519(this.identity.identityKeyPair.privateKey);\n    const ownSignedPrekey = this.identity.signedPrekeyPair.privateKey;\n\n    // Each exchange mirrors the initiator's, and the ordering must match\n    // exactly, since the derivation hashes their concatenation.\n    const contributions: Uint8Array[] = [\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownSignedPrekey, peerIdentityX),\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownIdentityX, peerEphemeral),\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownSignedPrekey, peerEphemeral),\n    ];\n\n    if (parsed.opk !== null) {\n      const oneTime = this.identity.oneTimePrekeys[parsed.opk];\n      if (oneTime === undefined) {\n        throw new Error(`One-time prekey ${parsed.opk} is not held by this client`);\n      }\n      contributions.push(sodium.crypto_scalarmult(oneTime.privateKey, peerEphemeral));\n    }\n\n    // Decapsulation cannot report a corrupted ciphertext: a wrong value simply\n    // produces a different
