A peer's frame is not trusted to be well formed. */\nexport function parseHybridHeader(header: Uint8Array): HybridHandshakeHeader {\n  let parsed: unknown;\n  try {\n    parsed = JSON.parse(sodium.to_string(header));\n  } catch {\n    throw new Error('Hybrid handshake header is not valid JSON');\n  }\n\n  if (typeof parsed !== 'object' || parsed === null) {\n    throw new Error('Hybrid handshake header is not an object');\n  }\n\n  const candidate = parsed as Record<string, unknown>;\n\n  if (candidate.v !== 1) {\n    throw new Error(`Unsupported handshake header version: ${String(candidate.v)}`);\n  }\n  if (candidate.mode !== 'hybrid') {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header does not name the hybrid agreement');\n  }\n  if (typeof candidate.ik !== 'string' || typeof candidate.ek !== 'string') {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header is missing key material');\n  }\n  if (typeof candidate.ct !== 'string') {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header carries no encapsulation');\n  }\n  if (candidate.opk !== null && typeof candidate.opk !== 'number') {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header carries an invalid prekey identifier');\n  }\n\n  // Checked before the value reaches the library, so a malformed header is\n  // rejected with a useful message rather than failing inside WebAssembly.\n  const ciphertextLength = fromBase64(candidate.ct).length;\n  if (ciphertextLength !== ML_KEM_768_CIPHERTEXT_BYTES) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `Handshake header carries a ${ciphertextLength}-byte encapsulation; ` +\n        `ML-KEM-768 produces ${ML_KEM_768_CIPHERTEXT_BYTES}`\n    );\n  }\n\n  return {\n    v: 1,\n    mode: 'hybrid',\n    ik: candidate.ik,\n    ek: candidate.ek,\n    opk: candidate.opk,\n    ct: candidate.ct,\n  };\n}\n", "/**\n * The Double Ratchet key schedule (Perrin and Marlinspike, 2016).\n *\n * This module contains only the two key derivation functions the ratchet is\n * built from. Both are pure: they take key material and return key material,\n * hold no state, and reach nothing outside themselves. The state machine that\n * drives them belongs to the sending and receiving logic and is introduced\n * separately.\n *\n * WHY TWO CHAINS\n *\n * A session key that is fixed for the lifetime of a conversation, as used up\n * to this point, means that compromise of that key exposes every message in\n * the conversation, past and future. The ratchet removes both halves of that\n * exposure, using a different mechanism for each.\n *\n * The symmetric chain gives forward secrecy per message. Each message\n * consumes a message key derived from the current chain key, and the chain\n * key is then replaced by its own successor. Because the derivation is\n * one-way, an adversary who seizes the chain key as it stands can derive\n * every future message key but cannot run the chain backwards to recover a\n * single earlier one. Deleting each message key after use is therefore not\n * housekeeping: it is the property itself.\n *\n * The root chain gives break-in recovery, and is advanced by mixing in a\n * fresh Diffie-Hellman output rather than by hashing alone. Hashing alone\n * cannot recover from compromise, since an adversary holding the current\n * state can compute every subsequent state. Introducing new entropy that the\n * adversary did not observe restores secrecy for messages sent afterwards.\n * The Diffie-Hellman ratchet that supplies that entropy is built on top of\n * this function.\n *\n * CONSTRUCTION\n *\n * The specification prescribes HMAC-SHA256. libsodium does not expose it\n * (`crypto_auth` is HMAC-SHA512-256), so keyed BLAKE2b is used, consistently\n * with the handshake. BLAKE2b is a secure pseudo-random function and the\n * construction preserves what the ratchet requires: outputs indistinguishable\n * from random without the key, and no way to invert them. As with the\n * handshake, the consequence is that this implementation is not wire\n * compatible with Signal.\n *\n * Each derivation uses the current key as the BLAKE2b key and a distinct\n * constant label as the message. Two outputs drawn from the same key are\n * therefore independent: recovering a message key reveals nothing about the\n * chain key that follows it, which would otherwise collapse the whole chain.\n *\n * `initSodium()` must have resolved before any function here is called.\n */\n\nimport { sodium } from './crypto';\n\n/** Chain keys, root keys and message keys are all 32 bytes. */\nexport const KEY_BYTES = 32;\n\n/**\n * Domain separators.\n *\n * Signal distinguishes the two chain outputs with single constant bytes. Full\n * labels are used here instead: they cost nothing, they cannot collide with\n * the labels used by the handshake or the message keys, and they make the\n * derivation self-describing when the code is read.\n *\n * Each must be at least crypto_generichash_KEYBYTES_MIN (16) bytes, since the\n * same strings are used as BLAKE2b keys in the root derivation.\n */\nconst MESSAGE_KEY_LABEL = 'Burblit-ratchet-message-key-v1';\nconst NEXT_CHAIN_KEY_LABEL = 'Burblit-ratchet-chain-next-v1';\nconst ROOT_KEY_LABEL = 'Burblit-ratchet-root-next-v1';\nconst ROOT_CHAIN_LABEL = 'Burblit-ratchet-root-chain-v1';\n\nexport interface ChainStep {\n  /** Replaces the chain key that produced it. The old value must be discarded. */\n  nextChainKey: Uint8Array;\n\n  /** Encrypts exactly one message, and is deleted immediately afterwards. */\n  messageKey: Uint8Array;\n}\n\nexport interface RootStep {\n  /** Replaces the root key that produced it. */\n  nextRootKey: Uint8Array;\n\n  /** Seeds a fresh symmetric chain for the new Diffie-Hellman ratchet step. */\n  chainKey: Uint8Array;\n}\n\n/**\n * Advances a symmetric chain by one step.\n *\n * Returns the message key for the current position and the chain key for the\n * next. The caller is responsible for discarding the chain key passed in:\n * retaining it would let anyone who later obtained it rederive every message\n * key from that point onwards, which is precisely what advancing the chain is\n * meant to prevent.\n */\nexport function advanceChain(chainKey: Uint8Array): ChainStep {\n  return {\n    messageKey: sodium.crypto_generichash(\n      KEY_BYTES,\n      sodium.from_string(MESSAGE_KEY_LABEL),\n      chainKey\n    ),\n    nextChainKey: sodium.crypto_generichash(\n      KEY_BYTES,\n      sodium.from_string(NEXT_CHAIN_KEY_LABEL),\n      chainKey\n    ),\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * Advances the root chain by one Diffie-Hellman ratchet step.\n *\n * The new entropy is the Diffie-Hellman output; the current root key provides\n * continuity with everything that came before. Both are required. Without the\n * Diffie-Hellman output the chain could be run forward by anyone holding the\n * current state, so break-in recovery would be lost; without the root key the\n * chain would have no history and a single compromised exchange would be\n * enough to reconstruct it.\n *\n * The root key is supplied as the BLAKE2b key and the Diffie-Hellman output as\n * part of the message, so neither can be varied independently to steer the\n * result.\n */\nexport function advanceRoot(rootKey: Uint8Array, dhOutput: Uint8Array): RootStep {\n  const nextRootKey = sodium.crypto_generichash(\n    KEY_BYTES,\n    concat(sodium.from_string(ROOT_KEY_LABEL), dhOutput),\n    rootKey\n  );\n\n  const chainKey = sodium.crypto_generichash(\n    KEY_BYTES,\n    concat(sodium.from_string(ROOT_CHAIN_LABEL), dhOutput),\n    rootKey\n  );\n\n  return { nextRootKey, chainKey };\n}\n\nfunction concat(left: Uint8Array, right: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {\n  const combined = new Uint8Array(left.length + right.length);\n  combined.set(left, 0);\n  combined.set(right, left.length);\n  return combined;\n}\n\n/**\n * Overwrites key material in place.\n *\n * JavaScript offers no guarantee that this prevents the bytes from surviving\n * elsewhere: the engine may have copied the array during garbage collection,\n * and nothing stops the operating system from having paged it out. It is\n * called where a key is discarded because it narrows the window in which a\n * value is readable, and because the absence of any attempt at erasure would\n * be a worse default. The limitation is recorded rather than papered over.\n */\nexport function wipe(key: Uint8Array): void {\n  key.fill(0);\n}\n", "/**\n * A simplified X3DH key agreement (Marlinspike and Perrin, 2016).\n *\n * The protocol exists to solve a problem a plain Diffie-Hellman cannot: two\n * parties must reach a shared secret when only one of them is online. The\n * recipient publishes key material in advance; the initiator collects it from\n * the registry, derives the root key alone, and sends a header from which the\n * recipient reconstructs the same value whenever it next connects.\n *\n * Three Diffie-Hellman exchanges are combined, and each contributes a distinct\n * property:\n *\n *   DH1 = DH(IK_initiator, SPK_recipient)  authenticates the initiator\n *   DH2 = DH(EK_initiator, IK_recipient)   authenticates the recipient\n *   DH3 = DH(EK_initiator, SPK_recipient)  provides forward secrecy\n *   DH4 = DH(EK_initiator, OPK_recipient)  optional; strengthens DH3\n *\n * DH1 and DH2 each bind one party's long-term identity into the result, so a\n * passive adversary holding neither identity key cannot reach the root key.\n * DH3 and DH4 involve an ephemeral key the initiator discards immediately, so\n * compromise of both identity keys at some later date does not reveal the root\n * key of a session already completed. Concatenating all four and hashing once\n * means every contribution must be correct: an adversary able to compute three\n * of them still learns nothing.\n *\n * DH4 is omitted when the recipient's one-time prekey pool is exhausted, which\n * the specification permits. The session then rests on the signed prekey\n * alone, which is reused across handshakes, so forward secrecy for the initial\n * message is weaker until the ratchet advances.\n *\n * DIVERGENCES FROM THE SPECIFICATION, both recorded in the design chapter:\n *\n * 1. X3DH specifies HKDF-SHA256 for the derivation. libsodium-wrappers does\n *    not reliably expose HKDF, so keyed BLAKE2b is used instead. BLAKE2b is a\n *    secure pseudo-random function and the construction preserves the property\n *    required here, that the output is indistinguishable from random without\n *    knowledge of every input. It is a substitution of primitive, not a\n *    weakening of the construction, but it does mean this implementation is\n *    not wire-compatible with Signal.\n *\n * 2. The associated data that X3DH binds into the derivation, carrying both\n *    parties' identity keys, is included here as the BLAKE2b key rather than\n *    as a separate HKDF info parameter. The binding is preserved; its position\n *    in the construction differs.\n */\n\nimport {\n  ROOT_KEY_BYTES,\n  type AgreementMode,\n  type InitiationResult,\n  type KeyAgreement,\n  type ResponseResult,\n} from '../shared/keyAgreement';\nimport type { PrekeyBundle } from '../shared/protocol';\nimport { initSodium, sodium, toBase64, fromBase64 } from './crypto';\nimport {\n  identityPrivateToX25519,\n  identityPublicToX25519,\n  verifyBundle,\n  type Identity,\n} from './identity';\n\n/**\n * The handshake header.\n *\n * Travels with the first message and is opaque to the relay. It carries no\n * secret: the ephemeral public key is public by construction, and the prekey\n * identifier only names which published key was consumed.\n */\nexport interface HandshakeHeader {\n  /** Protocol version, so a future hybrid header is distinguishable. */\n  v: 1;\n  mode: AgreementMode;\n  /** Initiator's Ed25519 identity key, base64. */\n  ik: string;\n  /** Initiator's ephemeral X25519 public key, base64. */\n  ek: string;\n  /** Identifier of the consumed one-time prekey, or null if none was served. */\n  opk: number | null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Domain separator, so these derivations cannot collide with the ratchet's.\n * Used as a BLAKE2b key, which libsodium requires to be at least\n * crypto_generichash_KEYBYTES_MIN (16) bytes long.\n */\nconst DERIVATION_CONTEXT = 'Burblit-X3DH-v1-root';\n\n/**\n * Derives the root key from the concatenated Diffie-Hellman outputs.\n *\n * `associatedData` binds both identity keys into the result, which is what\n * prevents an unknown-key-share attack: an adversary who relays a handshake\n * while substituting identities produces a different root key on each side,\n * so the session fails rather than succeeding under a false identity.\n */\nfunction deriveRootKey(dhOutputs: Uint8Array[], associatedData: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {\n  const totalLength = dhOutputs.reduce((sum, part) => sum + part.length, 0);\n  const concatenated = new Uint8Array(totalLength);\n\n  let offset = 0;\n  for (const part of dhOutputs) {\n    concatenated.set(part, offset);\n    offset += part.length;\n  }\n\n  // BLAKE2b accepts a key of at most crypto_generichash_KEYBYTES_MAX; the\n  // associated data is hashed down to a fixed length first so that identity\n  // keys of any size remain usable.\n  const derivationKey = sodium.crypto_generichash(\n    sodium.crypto_generichash_KEYBYTES,\n    associatedData,\n    sodium.from_string(DERIVATION_CONTEXT)\n  );\n\n  return sodium.crypto_generichash(ROOT_KEY_BYTES, concatenated, derivationKey);\n}\n\n/** Concatenates both identity keys in a fixed order, initiator first. */\nfunction associatedData(initiatorIdentity: Uint8Array, responderIdentity: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {\n  const combined = new Uint8Array(initiatorIdentity.length + responderIdentity.length);\n  combined.set(initiatorIdentity, 0);\n  combined.set(responderIdentity, initiatorIdentity.length);\n  return combined;\n}\n\nexport class X25519Agreement implements KeyAgreement {\n  readonly mode: AgreementMode = 'x25519';\n\n  constructor(private readonly identity: Identity) {}\n\n  /**\n   * Derives a root key as the initiating party.\n   *\n   * The bundle's signature is verified first. Skipping that check would let\n   * the relay serve its own signed prekey in place of the recipient's and\n   * decrypt everything that followed, which is precisely the attack\n   * end-to-end encryption exists to prevent.\n   */\n  async initiate(peerBundle: PrekeyBundle): Promise<InitiationResult> {\n    await initSodium();\n\n    if (!(await verifyBundle(peerBundle))) {\n      throw new Error(\n        `Bundle for ${peerBundle.username} failed signature verification; refusing to proceed`\n      );\n    }\n\n    const peerIdentityEd = fromBase64(peerBundle.identityKey);\n    const peerIdentityX = identityPublicToX25519(peerIdentityEd);\n    const peerSignedPrekey = fromBase64(peerBundle.signedPrekey);\n\n    // Ephemeral keypair. Its private half is never stored and goes out of\n    // scope when this function returns, which is what forward secrecy rests\n    // on: there is nothing left to seize.\n    const ephemeral = sodium.crypto_box_keypair();\n\n    const ownIdentityX = identityPrivateToX25519(this.identity.identityKeyPair.privateKey);\n\n    const dhOutputs: Uint8Array[] = [\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownIdentityX, peerSignedPrekey),\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ephemeral.privateKey, peerIdentityX),\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ephemeral.privateKey, peerSignedPrekey),\n    ];\n\n    if (peerBundle.oneTimePrekey !== null) {\n      dhOutputs.push(\n        sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ephemeral.privateKey, fromBase64(peerBundle.oneTimePrekey))\n      );\n    }\n\n    const rootKey = deriveRootKey(\n      dhOutputs,\n      associatedData(this.identity.identityKeyPair.publicKey, peerIdentityEd)\n    );\n\n    const header: HandshakeHeader = {\n      v: 1,\n      mode: this.mode,\n      ik: toBase64(this.identity.identityKeyPair.publicKey),\n      ek: toBase64(ephemeral.publicKey),\n      opk: peerBundle.oneTimePrekeyId,\n    };\n\n    return {\n      rootKey,\n      header: sodium.from_string(JSON.stringify(header)),\n    };\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Derives the same root key as the responding party.\n   *\n   * Every exchange is the mirror of the initiator's: the private key the\n   * initiator used a public counterpart of, and vice versa. The ordering of\n   * the four outputs must match exactly, since the derivation hashes their\n   * concatenation.\n   */\n  async respond(header: Uint8Array): Promise<ResponseResult> {\n    await initSodium();\n\n    const parsed = parseHandshakeHeader(header);\n\n    const peerIdentityEd = fromBase64(parsed.ik);\n    const peerIdentityX = identityPublicToX25519(peerIdentityEd);\n    const peerEphemeral = fromBase64(parsed.ek);\n\n    const ownIdentityX = identityPrivateToX25519(this.identity.identityKeyPair.privateKey);\n    const ownSignedPrekey = this.identity.signedPrekeyPair.privateKey;\n\n    const dhOutputs: Uint8Array[] = [\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownSignedPrekey, peerIdentityX),\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownIdentityX, peerEphemeral),\n      sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownSignedPrekey, peerEphemeral),\n    ];\n\n    if (parsed.opk !== null) {\n      const oneTime = this.identity.oneTimePrekeys[parsed.opk];\n      if (oneTime === undefined) {\n        // The prekey was consumed by an earlier handshake, or belongs to a\n        // bundle this client has since replaced. Either way the exchange\n        // cannot be completed, and failing loudly is correct: proceeding\n        // would silently derive a different key and corrupt the session.\n        throw new Error(`One-time prekey ${parsed.opk} is not held by this client`);\n      }\n      dhOutputs.push(sodium.crypto_scalarmult(oneTime.privateKey, peerEphemeral));\n    }\n\n    const rootKey = deriveRootKey(\n      dhOutputs,\n      associatedData(peerIdentityEd, this.identity.identityKeyPair.publicKey)\n    );\n\n    return { rootKey };\n  }\n}\n\n/** Narrows an untrusted header. A peer's frame is not trusted to be well formed. */\nexport function parseHandshakeHeader(header: Uint8Array): HandshakeHeader {\n  let parsed: unknown;\n  try {\n    parsed = JSON.parse(sodium.to_string(header));\n  } catch {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header is not valid JSON');\n  }\n\n  if (typeof parsed !== 'object' || parsed === null) {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header is not an object');\n  }\n\n  const candidate = parsed as Record<string, unknown>;\n\n  if (candidate.v !== 1) {\n    throw new Error(`Unsupported handshake header version: ${String(candidate.v)}`);\n  }\n  if (candidate.mode !== 'x25519' && candidate.mode !== 'hybrid') {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header names an unknown agreement mode');\n  }\n  if (typeof candidate.ik !== 'string' || typeof candidate.ek !== 'string') {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header is missing key material');\n  }\n  if (candidate.opk !== null && typeof candidate.opk !== 'number') {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header carries an invalid prekey identifier');\n  }\n\n  return {\n    v: 1,\n    mode: candidate.mode,\n    ik: candidate.ik,\n    ek: candidate.ek,\n    opk: candidate.opk,\n  };\n}\n\n/** Fetches a correspondent's bundle from the registry. */\nexport async function fetchPeerBundle(username: string): Promise<PrekeyBundle> {\n  const response = await fetch(`/keys/${encodeURIComponent(username)}`);\n  if (response.status === 404) {\n    throw new Error(`${username} has not published key material`);\n  }\n  if (!response.ok) {\n    throw new Error(`Bundle request failed with status ${response.status}`);\n  }\n  return (await response.json()) as PrekeyBundle;\n}\n", "/**\n * Encrypted sessions, driven by the Double Ratchet.\n *\n * TWO RATCHETS, TWO PROPERTIES\n *\n * The symmetric ratchet advances a chain by hashing, producing one key per\n * message and discarding it afterwards. That gives forward secrecy: an\n * adversary holding the chain as it stands cannot run it backwards to recover\n * a message already sent. What it cannot give is recovery from compromise,\n * because hashing is deterministic and an adversary holding the current state\n * can compute every state that follows it, indefinitely.\n *\n * The Diffie-Hellman ratchet supplies what hashing cannot: entropy the\n * adversary did not observe. Each party holds a ratchet keypair and advertises\n * its public half on every message. When a party observes that its\n * correspondent's ratchet key has changed, it performs a ratchet step,\n * deriving a fresh receiving chain from its own private key and the new public\n * key, then generating a new keypair and deriving a fresh sending chain.\n *\n * MODE NEGOTIATION\n *\n * The root key that seeds all of this comes from one of two agreements, and\n * the ratchet cannot tell which: both satisfy the same interface and both\n * yield 32 bytes. The initiator chooses, guided by a policy and by what the\n * correspondent has published; the responder is told, by the mode named in\n * the handshake header.\n *\n * The policy exists for two reasons. The first is measurement: the same\n * conversation can be run under either agreement and the difference observed,\n * which is what makes the comparison empirical rather than estimated. The\n * second is the downgrade problem. An active relay can strip the post-quantum\n * key from a bundle it serves, and no signature prevents that, because a\n * signature protects what a bundle contains and not what it omits. A client\n * that requires the hybrid agreement therefore fails rather than proceeding,\n * which converts a silent degradation into a visible error. That is the only\n * defence available without an authenticated binding between names and keys,\n * and it is recorded as such in the threat model.\n *\n * OUT-OF-ORDER DELIVERY\n *\n * A chain can only be advanced forwards, so keys for positions skipped over\n * are retained rather than discarded, indexed by the ratchet key and message\n * number that identify them, and consumed when the message they belong to\n * arrives. The same applies across a ratchet step: the header states how long\n * the superseded sending chain was, so the remainder of the corresponding\n * receiving chain is derived and stored before the step is taken.\n *\n * The store is bounded, because an adversary could otherwise force unbounded\n * allocation by sending messages with ever-increasing numbers. When full it\n * evicts the oldest entry: a message missing for a long time is the one least\n * likely still to arrive, and degrading is better than refusing to serve.\n *\n * LIMITATIONS\n *\n * Retained keys weaken forward secrecy for exactly the messages they belong\n * to, which is the unavoidable cost of tolerating loss and is bounded by the\n * size of the store. Sessions are held in memory and lost on reload.\n */\n\nimport type { AgreementMode, KeyAgreement } from '../shared/keyAgreement';\nimport { toBase64, fromBase64, initSodium, sodium } from './crypto';\nimport { HybridAgreement, supportsHybrid } from './hybridAgreement';\nimport type { Identity, KeyPair } from './identity';\nimport { advanceChain, advanceRoot, wipe } from './ratchet';\nimport { X25519Agreement, fetchPeerBundle } from './x25519Agreement';\n\nconst NONCE_BYTES = 12;\n\n/**\n * How far a single message may advance a receiving chain.\n *\n * Without a bound, a message claiming an arbitrarily large number would\n * compel the recipient to perform that many derivations before discovering it\n * could not decrypt the result, which is a denial of service requiring one\n * forged frame.\n */\nexport const MAX_SKIPPED_MESSAGES = 1000;\n\n/** How many skipped message keys a session retains across all chains. */\nexport const MAX_STORED_KEYS = 2000;\n\nexport type SessionRole = 'initiator' | 'responder';\n\n/**\n * Which agreement a client will use.\n *\n * `auto` takes the hybrid agreement where the correspondent has published the\n * material for it. The two explicit values force the choice: `hybrid` refuses\n * to proceed without post-quantum protection rather than falling back, and\n * `x25519` is what allows a conversation to be run classically for comparison.\n */\nexport type ModePolicy = 'auto' | 'hybrid' | 'x25519';\n\nlet modePolicy: ModePolicy = 'auto';\n\nexport function setModePolicy(policy: ModePolicy): void {\n  modePolicy = policy;\n}\n\nexport function getModePolicy(): ModePolicy {\n  return modePolicy;\n}\n\nexport interface Session {\n  peer: string;\n  role: SessionRole;\n\n  /** Which agreement produced this session's root key. */\n  agreementMode: AgreementMode;\n\n  /** The root chain, advanced by every Diffie-Hellman ratchet step. */\n  rootKey: Uint8Array;\n\n  /** This party's current ratchet keypair. Replaced at every step. */\n  ratchetKeyPair: KeyPair;\n\n  /** The correspondent's current ratchet public key, base64. */\n  remoteRatchetKey: string | null;\n\n  /** Null until a sending chain exists, which for a responder means until\n   *  the first message has been received. */\n  sendingChainKey: Uint8Array | null;\n\n  /** Null until the correspondent's first message establishes it. */\n  receivingChainKey: Uint8Array | null;\n\n  /** Position within the current sending chain. */\n  sendCounter: number;\n\n  /** Position within the current receiving chain. */\n  receiveCounter: number;\n\n  /** Length of the sending chain superseded by the last ratchet step. */\n  previousSendingChainLength: number;\n\n  /**\n   * Keys for positions the receiving chains have passed without a message\n   * arriving, indexed by `ratchetKey:messageNumber`. Insertion order is\n   * meaningful: the oldest entry is evicted when the store is full.\n   */\n  skippedKeys: Map<string, Uint8Array>;\n\n  /**\n   * The handshake header, held by the initiator and attached to every message\n   * it sends, so that a responder which has lost its session can re-derive\n   * the root key rather than being unable to read anything further.\n   */\n  handshakeHeader: string | null;\n}\n\n/** The per-message header, carried alongside the ciphertext. */\nexport interface MessageHeader {\n  v: 1;\n  /** Handshake header, present while the initiator keeps resending it. */\n  hs?: string;\n  /** The sender's current ratchet public key, base64. */\n  dh: string;\n  /** Length of the sender's previous sending chain. */\n  pn: number;\n  /** Position of this message within the sender's current sending chain. */\n  n: number;\n}\n\nexport interface OutgoingMessage {\n  /** Base64 ciphertext. */\n  payload: string;\n  /** Base64 of the encoded message header. */\n  header: string;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Encoding helpers\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nfunction diffieHellman(ownPrivate: Uint8Array, peerPublic: string): Uint8Array {\n  return sodium.crypto_scalarmult(ownPrivate, fromBase64(peerPublic));\n}\n\nfunction encodeHeader(header: MessageHeader): string {\n  return toBase64(sodium.from_string(JSON.stringify(header)));\n}\n\n/** Narrows an untrusted header. A peer's frame is not trusted to be well formed. */\nexport function decodeHeader(encoded: string): MessageHeader {\n  let parsed: unknown;\n  try {\n    parsed = JSON.parse(sodium.to_string(fromBase64(encoded)));\n  } catch {\n    throw new Error('Message header is not valid encoded JSON');\n  }\n\n  if (typeof parsed !== 'object' || parsed === null) {\n    throw new Error('Message header is not an object');\n  }\n\n  const candidate = parsed as Record<string, unknown>;\n\n  if (candidate.v !== 1) {\n    throw new Error(`Unsupported message header version: ${String(candidate.v)}`);\n  }\n  if (typeof candidate.dh !== 'string') {\n    throw new Error('Message header carries no ratchet key');\n  }\n  if (typeof candidate.pn !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(candidate.pn) || candidate.pn < 0) {\n    throw new Error('Message header carries an invalid previous chain length');\n  }\n  if (typeof candidate.n !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(candidate.n) || candidate.n < 0) {\n    throw new Error('Message header carries an invalid message number');\n  }\n  if (candidate.hs !== undefined && typeof candidate.hs !== 'string') {\n    throw new Error('Message header carries an invalid handshake header');\n  }\n\n  return {\n    v: 1,\n    dh: candidate.dh,\n    pn: candidate.pn,\n    n: candidate.n,\n    ...(typeof candidate.hs === 'string' ? { hs: candidate.hs } : {}),\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * Extracts the handshake header a message carries, if any.\n *\n * Used before a session exists, to decide whether an incoming message can\n * establish one.\n */\nexport function handshakeHeaderFrom(encoded: string): string | null {\n  try {\n    return decodeHeader(encoded).hs ?? null;\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Reads the agreement named by a handshake header.\n *\n * Both headers declare their own mode, which is what lets the responder\n * select the matching implementation without having to guess from the\n * structure. An unrecognised value is an error rather than a default: silently\n * treating an unknown mode as classical would be a downgrade performed by the\n * recipient on itself.\n */\nexport function handshakeMode(encodedHandshakeHeader: string): AgreementMode {\n  let parsed: unknown;\n  try {\n    parsed = JSON.parse(sodium.to_string(fromBase64(encodedHandshakeHeader)));\n  } catch {\n    throw new Error('Handshake header is not valid encoded JSON');\n  }\n\n  const mode = (parsed as Record<string, unknown> | null)?.mode;\n  if (mode !== 'x25519' && mode !== 'hybrid') {\n    throw new Error(`Handshake header names an unknown agreement: ${String(mode)}`);\n  }\n  return mode;\n}\n\nfunction nonceFromCounter(counter: number): Uint8Array {\n  const nonce = new Uint8Array(NONCE_BYTES);\n  new DataView(nonce.buffer).setBigUint64(NONCE_BYTES - 8, BigInt(counter), false);\n  return nonce;\n}\n\n/**\n * Builds the associated data for a message.\n *\n * The ratchet key, chain length and message number are included because they\n * determine which keys the recipient derives; altering any of them must\n * invalidate the message rather than silently redirect the derivation. The\n * handshake header is excluded: removing it can only prevent a session from\n * being established, and including it would break authentication for messages\n * sent after the initiator stops resending it.\n */\nfunction associatedData(from: string, to: string, header: MessageHeader): Uint8Array {\n  return sodium.from_string(`${from}:${to}:${header.dh}:${header.pn}:${header.n}`);\n}\n\nfunction skippedKeyIndex(ratchetKey: string, messageNumber: number): string {\n  return `${ratchetKey}:${messageNumber}`;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Session construction\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * Builds the initiating party's session.\n *\n * The recipient's signed prekey serves as its first ratchet public key, so a\n * sending ratchet step can be performed at once and the first message\n * encrypted without the recipient ever having been online.\n */\nexport function initiatorSession(\n  peer: string,\n  rootKey: Uint8Array,\n  peerSignedPrekey: string,\n  handshakeHeader: string,\n  agreementMode: AgreementMode = 'x25519'\n): Session {\n  const ratchetKeyPair = sodium.crypto_box_keypair();\n  const step = advanceRoot(rootKey, diffieHellman(ratchetKeyPair.privateKey, peerSignedPrekey));\n\n  return {\n    peer,\n    role: 'initiator',\n    agreementMode,\n    rootKey: step.nextRootKey,\n    ratchetKeyPair,\n    remoteRatchetKey: peerSignedPrekey,\n    sendingChainKey: step.chainKey,\n    receivingChainKey: null,\n    sendCounter: 0,\n    receiveCounter: 0,\n    previousSendingChainLength: 0,\n    skippedKeys: new Map(),\n    handshakeHeader,\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * Builds the responding party's session.\n *\n * It begins with no chains. Its signed prekey pair is its first ratchet\n * keypair, which is the pair the initiator has already performed a\n * Diffie-Hellman against; both chains are derived when the first message\n * arrives and the ratchet step is performed.\n */\nexport function responderSession(\n  peer: string,\n  rootKey: Uint8Array,\n  signedPrekeyPair: KeyPair,\n  agreementMode: AgreementMode = 'x25519'\n): Session {\n  return {\n    peer,\n    role: 'responder',\n    agreementMode,\n    rootKey,\n    ratchetKeyPair: signedPrekeyPair,\n    remoteRatchetKey: null,\n    sendingChainKey: null,\n    receivingChainKey: null,\n    sendCounter: 0,\n    receiveCounter: 0,\n    previousSendingChainLength: 0,\n    skippedKeys: new Map(),\n    handshakeHeader: null,\n  };\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Session registry\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nconst sessions = new Map<string, Session>();\n\n/** Discards all sessions, for example when the identity or the policy changes. */\nexport function clearSessions(): void {\n  sessions.clear();\n}\n\n/** Returns an established session, or null when none exists. */\nexport function existingSession(peer: string): Session | null {\n  return sessions.get(peer) ?? null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Decides which agreement an outgoing handshake will use.\n *\n * Kept as a pure function of the policy and what the correspondent has\n * published, so the rule can be checked exhaustively without a network or a\n * registry. The decision is small but consequential: it is the point at which\n * a downgrade would occur if one were permitted.\n *\n * Refusing to fall back when the hybrid agreement is required is the only\n * protection available against a relay that strips post-quantum material from\n * a bundle, because a signature covers what the bundle contains and not what\n * it lacks.\n */\nexport function chooseMode(\n  policy: ModePolicy,\n  hybridAvailable: boolean,\n  peer: string\n): AgreementMode {\n  if (policy === 'x25519') {\n    return 'x25519';\n  }\n  if (policy === 'hybrid') {\n    if (!hybridAvailable) {\n      throw new Error(\n        `${peer} has published no post-quantum key, and this client is configured to ` +\n          'require the hybrid agreement; refusing to fall back'\n      );\n    }\n    return 'hybrid';\n  }\n  return hybridAvailable ? 'hybrid' : 'x25519';\n}\n\nfunction agreementFor(identity: Identity, mode: AgreementMode): KeyAgreement {\n  return mode === 'hybrid' ? new HybridAgreement(identity) : new X25519Agreement(identity);\n}\n\n/**\n * Returns the session with a correspondent, establishing one if necessary.\n *\n * Called when this party sends first, so it takes the initiating role.\n */\nexport async function sessionAsInitiator(identity: Identity, peer: string): Promise<Session> {\n  const existing = sessions.get(peer);\n  if (existing !== undefined) {\n    return existing;\n  }\n\n  await initSodium();\n\n  const bundle = await fetchPeerBundle(peer);\n  const agreement = agreementFor(identity, chooseMode(modePolicy, supportsHybrid(bundle), peer));\n  const { rootKey, header } = await agreement.initiate(bundle);\n\n  const session = initiatorSession(\n    peer,\n    rootKey,\n    bundle.signedPrekey,\n    toBase64(header),\n    agreement.mode\n  );\n  sessions.set(peer, session);\n  return session;\n}\n\n/**\n * Returns the session implied by a received handshake header, establishing one\n * if necessary.\n *\n * The header is compared against the session already held, so a correspondent\n * that begins a new handshake, for instance after clearing its own state,\n * replaces the session rather than being unable to communicate.\n */\nexport async function sessionAsResponder(\n  identity: Identity,\n  peer: string,\n  handshakeHeader: string\n): Promise<Session> {\n  const existing = sessions.get(peer);\n  if (existing !== undefined && existing.handshakeHeader === handshakeHeader) {\n    return existing;\n  }\n\n  await initSodium();\n\n  const mode = handshakeMode(handshakeHeader);\n\n  // A client configured to require post-quantum protection rejects a\n  // classical handshake rather than answering it. Otherwise an adversary\n  // could downgrade a conversation simply by initiating it.\n  if (modePolicy === 'hybrid' && mode !== 'hybrid') {\n    throw new Error(\n      `${peer} opened a ${mode} session, and this client is configured to require ` +\n        'the hybrid agreement; refusing to answer'\n    );\n  }\n\n  const { rootKey } = await agreementFor(identity, mode).respond(fromBase64(handshakeHeader));\n\n  const session = responderSession(peer, rootKey, identity.signedPrekeyPair, mode);\n  session.handshakeHeader = handshakeHeader;\n  sessions.set(peer, session);\n  return session;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Sending\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport function encryptMessage(\n  session: Session,\n  plaintext: string,\n  from: string,\n  to: string\n): OutgoingMessage {\n  if (session.sendingChainKey === null) {\n    // A responder has no sending chain until the first message arrives and\n    // the ratchet step derives one.\n    throw new Error(`No sending chain established with ${session.peer} yet`);\n  }\n\n  const counter = session.sendCounter;\n  const { messageKey, nextChainKey } = advanceChain(session.sendingChainKey);\n\n  // The chain key just consumed is overwritten and replaced. Retaining it\n  // would let anyone who later obtained it rederive every message key from\n  // this point forward, which is what advancing the chain exists to prevent.\n  wipe(session.sendingChainKey);\n  session.sendingChainKey = nextChainKey;\n  session.sendCounter = counter + 1;\n\n  const header: MessageHeader = {\n    v: 1,\n    dh: toBase64(session.ratchetKeyPair.publicKey),\n    pn: session.previousSendingChainLength,\n    n: counter,\n    ...(session.handshakeHeader !== null ? { hs: session.handshakeHeader } : {}),\n  };\n\n  const ciphertext = sodium.crypto_aead_chacha20poly1305_ietf_encrypt(\n    sodium.from_string(plaintext),\n    associatedData(from, to, header),\n    null,\n    nonceFromCounter(counter),\n    messageKey\n  );\n\n  // The message key encrypts exactly one message and is of no further use.\n  wipe(messageKey);\n\n  return { payload: toBase64(ciphertext), header: encodeHeader(header) };\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Receiving\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nfunction decrypt(\n  messageKey: Uint8Array,\n  ciphertext: Uint8Array,\n  from: string,\n  to: string,\n  header: MessageHeader\n): string {\n  const plaintext = sodium.crypto_aead_chacha20poly1305_ietf_decrypt(\n    null,\n    ciphertext,\n    associatedData(from, to, header),\n    nonceFromCounter(header.n),\n    messageKey\n  );\n  return sodium.to_string(plaintext);\n}\n\n/**\n * Derives and retains the keys for positions a chain is about to pass.\n *\n * Returns the chain key the walk ended on. The caller holds the result aside\n * until the message authenticates, so a forged frame cannot populate the store\n * or move the chain.\n */\nfunction skipTo(\n  chainKey: Uint8Array,\n  from: number,\n  until: number,\n  ratchetKey: string,\n  into: Map<string, Uint8Array>\n): Uint8Array {\n  const distance = until - from;\n  if (distance > MAX_SKIPPED_MESSAGES) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `Advancing to message ${until} would skip ${distance} positions, beyond the ` +\n        `limit of ${MAX_SKIPPED_MESSAGES}; refusing to advance`\n    );\n  }\n\n  let current = chainKey;\n  for (let position = from; position < until; position += 1) {\n    const advanced = advanceChain(current);\n    into.set(skippedKeyIndex(ratchetKey, position), advanced.messageKey);\n    current = advanced.nextChainKey;\n  }\n  return current;\n}\n\n/**\n * Performs a Diffie-Hellman ratchet step against a new remote key.\n *\n * Two root steps are taken. The first, against this party's existing private\n * key, derives the receiving chain for the messages now arriving. The second,\n * against a freshly generated private key, derives the sending chain for the\n * replies. Generating that key here is what denies an adversary holding the\n * previous state any way to follow the conversation forward.\n */\nfunction ratchetStep(\n  rootKey: Uint8Array,\n  ownKeyPair: KeyPair,\n  remoteRatchetKey: string\n): {\n  rootKey: Uint8Array;\n  ratchetKeyPair: KeyPair;\n  receivingChainKey: Uint8Array;\n  sendingChainKey: Uint8Array;\n} {\n  const receiving = advanceRoot(rootKey, diffieHellman(ownKeyPair.privateKey, remoteRatchetKey));\n\n  const ratchetKeyPair = sodium.crypto_box_keypair();\n  const sending = advanceRoot(\n    receiving.nextRootKey,\n    diffieHellman(ratchetKeyPair.privateKey, remoteRatchetKey)\n  );\n\n  return {\n    rootKey: sending.nextRootKey,\n    ratchetKeyPair,\n    receivingChainKey: receiving.chainKey,\n    sendingChainKey: sending.chainKey,\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * Adds retained keys to the store, evicting the oldest entries once full.\n *\n * Eviction rather than refusal, because a message missing for a long time is\n * the one least likely still to arrive, and an adversary should not be able\n * to deny service simply by filling the store.\n */\nfunction retain(session: Session, additions: Map<string, Uint8Array>): void {\n  for (const [index, key] of additions) {\n    session.skippedKeys.set(index, key);\n  }\n\n  while (session.skippedKeys.size > MAX_STORED_KEYS) {\n    const oldest = session.skippedKeys.keys().next();\n    if (oldest.done === true) {\n      break;\n    }\n    const evicted = session.skippedKeys.get(oldest.value);\n    if (evicted !== undefined) {\n      wipe(evicted);\n    }\n    session.skippedKeys.delete(oldest.value);\n  }\n}\n\nexport function decryptMessage(\n  session: Session,\n  payload: string,\n  encodedHeader: string,\n  from: string,\n  to: string\n): string {\n  const header = decodeHeader(encodedHeader);\n  const ciphertext = fromBase64(payload);\n\n  // --- A message whose key was retained when the chain passed it ----------\n  const retainedIndex = skippedKeyIndex(header.dh, header.n);\n  const retained = session.skippedKeys.get(retainedIndex);\n  if (retained !== undefined) {\n    // Decrypted before the key is removed, so a message that fails to\n    // authenticate does not consume the key a genuine one will need.\n    const plaintext = decrypt(retained, ciphertext, from, to, header);\n    wipe(retained);\n    session.skippedKeys.delete(retainedIndex);\n    return plaintext;\n  }\n\n  // --- Otherwise the chains advance, on local values only -----------------\n  const pending = new Map<string, Uint8Array>();\n\n  let rootKey = session.rootKey;\n  let ratchetKeyPair = session.ratchetKeyPair;\n  let sendingChainKey = session.sendingChainKey;\n  let receivingChainKey: Uint8Array | null;\n  let receiveCounter: number;\n  let previousSendingChainLength = session.previousSendingChainLength;\n  let sendCounter = session.sendCounter;\n\n  if (header.dh !== session.remoteRatchetKey) {\n    // The superseded receiving chain may still owe messages. The header states\n    // how long it was, so its remaining keys are derived and retained before\n    // the chain is replaced and becomes underivable.\n    if (session.receivingChainKey !== null && session.remoteRatchetKey !== null) {\n      skipTo(\n        session.receivingChainKey,\n        session.receiveCounter,\n        header.pn,\n        session.remoteRatchetKey,\n        pending\n      );\n    }\n\n    const stepped = ratchetStep(session.rootKey, session.ratchetKeyPair, header.dh);\n    rootKey = stepped.rootKey;\n    ratchetKeyPair = stepped.ratchetKeyPair;\n    receivingChainKey = stepped.receivingChainKey;\n    sendingChainKey = stepped.sendingChainKey;\n\n    previousSendingChainLength = session.sendCounter;\n    sendCounter = 0;\n    receiveCounter = 0;\n  } else {\n    receivingChainKey = session.receivingChainKey;\n    receiveCounter = session.receiveCounter;\n  }\n\n  if (receivingChainKey === null) {\n    throw new Error('No receiving chain established, and this message does not begin one');\n  }\n\n  if (header.n < receiveCounter) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `Message ${header.n} is behind the current chain position (${receiveCounter}) ` +\n        'and its key was not retained'\n    );\n  }\n\n  // Positions between the current one and this message are retained, not\n  // discarded: that is what lets those messages be read if they arrive later.\n  const walked = skipTo(receivingChainKey, receiveCounter, header.n, header.dh, pending);\n  const { messageKey, nextChainKey } = advanceChain(walked);\n\n  let plaintext: string;\n  try {\n    plaintext = decrypt(messageKey, ciphertext, from, to, header);\n  } catch (error) {\n    // Nothing has been committed: the session and the store are untouched.\n    wipe(messageKey);\n    for (const key of pending.values()) {\n      wipe(key);\n    }\n    throw error;\n  }\n\n  wipe(messageKey);\n\n  if (session.receivingChainKey !== null) {\n    wipe(session.receivingChainKey);\n  }\n  if (session.sendingChainKey !== null && session.sendingChainKey !== sendingChainKey) {\n    wipe(session.sendingChainKey);\n  }\n\n  session.rootKey = rootKey;\n  session.ratchetKeyPair = ratchetKeyPair;\n  session.remoteRatchetKey = header.dh;\n  session.sendingChainKey = sendingChainKey;\n  session.receivingChainKey = nextChainKey;\n  session.sendCounter = sendCounter;\n  session.receiveCounter = header.n + 1;\n  session.previousSendingChainLength = previousSendingChainLength;\n  retain(session, pending);\n\n  return plaintext;\n}\n", "/**\n * Burblit browser client.\n *\n * All DOM nodes carrying untrusted data are built with `createElement` and\n * `textContent`. No string of remote origin is ever assigned to `innerHTML`,\n * and no inline event handler appears in the markup, so the application needs\n * no `unsafe-inline` allowance in a future Content Security Policy.\n */\n\nimport { createMLKEM768 } from '@oqs/liboqs-js';\n\nimport {\n  USERNAME_PATTERN,\n  MAX_TEXT_LENGTH,\n  type ClientMessage,\n  type ServerMessage,\n} from '../shared/protocol';\nimport { initSodium, toBase64, verifyX25519Exchange } from './crypto';\nimport { loadOrCreateIdentity, clearIdentity, type Identity } from './identity';\nimport { useMlKemFactory, verifyMlKemExchange } from './pqc';\nimport {\n  sessionAsInitiator,\n  sessionAsResponder,\n  existingSession,\n  encryptMessage,\n  decryptMessage,\n  handshakeHeaderFrom,\n  setModePolicy,\n  clearSessions,\n  type ModePolicy,\n  type Session,\n} from './session';\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// DOM access\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * Resolves a required element or fails loudly at start-up.\n *\n * Under `strict` every `getElementById` is nullable. Funnelling the lookups\n * through one checked helper keeps the rest of the module free of null guards\n * and turns a renamed id into an immediate, obvious failure.\n */\nfunction requireElement<T extends HTMLElement>(id: string): T {\n  const element = document.getElementById(id);\n  if (element === null) {\n    throw new Error(`Missing required element: #${id}`);\n  }\n  return element as T;\n}\n\nconst registerSection = requireElement<HTMLDivElement>('register-section');\nconst chatSection = requireElement<HTMLDivElement>('chat-section');\nconst usernameInput = requireElement<HTMLInputElement>('username-input');\nconst connectButton = requireElement<HTMLButtonElement>('connect-button');\nconst resetButton = requireElement<HTMLButtonElement>('reset-button');\nconst messagesPanel = requireElement<HTMLDivElement>('messages');\nconst recipientInput = requireElement<HTMLInputElement>('recipient-input');\nconst messageInput = requireElement<HTMLInputElement>('message-input');\nconst sendButton = requireElement<HTMLButtonElement>('send-button');\nconst modeSelect = requireElement<HTMLSelectElement>('mode-select');\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Session state\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nlet socket: WebSocket | null = null;\nlet connecting = false;\n\n/**\n * This client's long-term key material, loaded at connection time.\n *\n * Held at module scope because both halves of the handshake need it: the\n * initiator performs Diffie-Hellman with its identity key, and the responder\n * looks up the one-time prekey the initiator consumed.\n */\nlet identity: Identity | null = null;\n\n/**\n * Derives the WebSocket endpoint from the page origin, upgrading the scheme\n * when the page itself was served over TLS. Hard-coding `ws://localhost:3000`\n * would silently downgrade the transport once WSS is enabled.\n */\nfunction websocketUrl(): string {\n  const scheme = window.location.protocol === 'https:' ? 'wss:' : 'ws:';\n  return `${scheme}//${window.location.host}`;\n}\n\nfunction send(message: ClientMessage): void {\n  if (socket === null || socket.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) {\n    appendNotice('Not connected');\n    return;\n  }\n  socket.send(JSON.stringify(message));\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Rendering\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nfunction scrollToLatest(): void {\n  messagesPanel.scrollTop = messagesPanel.scrollHeight;\n}\n\n/** Renders a server notice or a local status line. */\nfunction appendNotice(text: string): void {\n  const line = document.createElement('div');\n  line.className = 'message info';\n  line.textContent = text;\n  messagesPanel.appendChild(line);\n  scrollToLatest();\n}\n\n/**\n * Renders a chat message. The sender name and the body are inserted as text\n * nodes, so markup arriving from a peer is displayed literally rather than\n * parsed. This is the mitigation for the stored-XSS vector that the previous\n * `innerHTML` concatenation opened up.\n */\nfunction appendChat(from: string, text: string): void {\n  const line = document.createElement('div');\n  line.className = 'message';\n\n  const sender = document.createElement('strong');\n  sender.textContent = `${from}: `;\n\n  line.appendChild(sender);\n  line.appendChild(document.createTextNode(text));\n  messagesPanel.appendChild(line);\n  scrollToLatest();\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Reporting the agreement in force\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/** Peers whose session mode has already been reported. */\nconst announced = new Set<string>();\n\n/**\n * States which agreement protects a conversation, once per correspondent.\n *\n * A security property the user cannot observe is not a property the user has.\n * The distinction matters most in the case this is designed to surface: a\n * conversation that silently fell back to the classical agreement looks\n * exactly like one that did not.\n */\nfunction announce(session: Session): void {\n  if (announced.has(session.peer)) {\n    return;\n  }\n  announced.add(session.peer);\n\n  appendNotice(\n    session.agreementMode === 'hybrid'\n      ? `Session with ${session.peer}: hybrid X25519 + ML-KEM-768`\n      : `Session with ${session.peer}: classical X25519 only`\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Inbound handling\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * Narrows a decoded frame to a known envelope. The server is not trusted to\n * send well-formed data simply because it is the server: a compromised or\n * buggy relay must not be able to drive the client into an undefined state.\n */\nfunction parseServerMessage(raw: string): ServerMessage | null {\n  let parsed: unknown;\n  try {\n    parsed = JSON.parse(raw);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  if (typeof parsed !== 'object' || parsed === null) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  const candidate = parsed as Record<string, unknown>;\n\n  if (candidate.type === 'info' && typeof candidate.text === 'string') {\n    return { type: 'info', text: candidate.text };\n  }\n  if (\n    candidate.type === 'error' &&\n    typeof candidate.code === 'string' &&\n    typeof candidate.text === 'string'\n  ) {\n    return { type: 'error', code: candidate.code, text: candidate.text };\n  }\n  if (\n    candidate.type === 'chat' &&\n    typeof candidate.from === 'string' &&\n    typeof candidate.text === 'string'\n  ) {\n    return {\n      type: 'chat',\n      from: candidate.from,\n      text: candidate.text,\n      ...(typeof candidate.header === 'string' ? { header: candidate.header } : {}),\n    };\n  }\n\n  return null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Establishes or recovers the session for an incoming message, then decrypts.\n *\n * A message carrying a handshake header can always be read, because the\n * header is sufficient to re-derive the root key. One that does not requires\n * a session already in memory, and a reload destroys those: the failure is\n * reported rather than hidden, since silently dropping a message the user's\n * correspondent believes was delivered would be worse than saying so.\n */\nasync function receive(from: string, payload: string, header?: string): Promise<void> {\n  if (identity === null) {\n    return;\n  }\n  if (header === undefined) {\n    appendNotice(`Discarded a message from ${from} that carried no header`);\n    return;\n  }\n\n  try {\n    const handshake = handshakeHeaderFrom(header);\n    const session =\n      handshake !== null\n        ? await sessionAsResponder(identity, from, handshake)\n        : existingSession(from);\n\n    if (session === null) {\n      appendNotice(`Cannot read a message from ${from}: no session established`);\n      return;\n    }\n\n    announce(session);\n    appendChat(from, decryptMessage(session, payload, header, from, identity.username));\n  } catch (error) {\n    // Reached when authentication fails, and also when a correspondent opens\n    // a classical session while this client requires the hybrid agreement.\n    console.error('Could not read message:', error);\n    appendNotice(`Message from ${from} was discarded: ${String(error)}`);\n  }\n}\n\nfunction handleServerMessage(message: ServerMessage): void {\n  switch (message.type) {\n    case 'info':\n      appendNotice(message.text);\n      break;\n    case 'error':\n      appendNotice(`Error (${message.code}): ${message.text}`);\n      break;\n    case 'chat':\n      void receive(message.from, message.text, message.header);\n      break;\n  }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Actions\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nasync function connect(): Promise<void> {\n  // The button is disabled on entry, but the Enter shortcut bypasses that, so\n  // re-entry is guarded explicitly rather than through the control's state.\n  // Concurrent entry matters here because publication is destructive: two\n  // overlapping runs would each replace the other's prekey batch.\n  if (connecting || socket !== null) {\n    return;\n  }\n  connecting = true;\n\n  const username = usernameInput.value.trim();\n\n  // Validated against the shared pattern before a frame is sent, so the user\n  // gets immediate feedback rather than a round trip and a server rejection.\n  if (!USERNAME_PATTERN.test(username)) {\n    window.alert('Username must be 3-20 characters: letters, digits, hyphen or underscore.');\n    connecting = false;\n    return;\n  }\n\n  connectButton.disabled = true;\n\n  // Key material must exist and be published before registering, so that a\n  // correspondent who fetches this user's bundle immediately afterwards finds\n  // something to fetch.\n  try {\n    identity = await loadOrCreateIdentity(username);\n  } catch (error) {\n    console.error('Identity setup failed:', error);\n    window.alert('Could not set up key material. See the console for details.');\n    connectButton.disabled = false;\n    connecting = false;\n    return;\n  }\n\n  console.log(\n    `Identity ready for ${username}.`,\n    `Fingerprint: ${toBase64(identity.identityKeyPair.publicKey).slice(0, 16)}...`,\n    `One-time prekeys held: ${Object.keys(identity.oneTimePrekeys).length}`\n  );\n\n  socket = new WebSocket(websocketUrl());\n\n  socket.addEventListener('open', () => {\n    send({ type: 'register', username });\n    registerSection.style.display = 'none';\n    chatSection.style.display = 'block';\n    messageInput.focus();\n  });\n\n  socket.addEventListener('message', (event: MessageEvent<string>) => {\n    const message = parseServerMessage(event.data);\n    if (message === null) {\n      console.warn('Discarded malformed frame from server');\n      return;\n    }\n    handleServerMessage(message);\n  });\n\n  socket.addEventListener('close', () => {\n    appendNotice('Disconnected from server');\n    socket = null;\n    connecting = false;\n    connectButton.disabled = false;\n  });\n\n  socket.addEventListener('error', () => {\n    appendNotice('Connection error');\n  });\n}\n\nasync function sendMessage(): Promise<void> {\n  const to = recipientInput.value.trim();\n  const text = messageInput.value.trim();\n\n  if (!USERNAME_PATTERN.test(to)) {\n    appendNotice('Invalid recipient username');\n    return;\n  }\n  if (text.length === 0) {\n    return;\n  }\n  // The plaintext bound is enforced here because the client is the only party\n  // that can see a plaintext; the server bounds the encoded payload instead.\n  if (text.length > MAX_TEXT_LENGTH) {\n    appendNotice(`Message too long (limit ${MAX_TEXT_LENGTH} characters)`);\n    return;\n  }\n  if (identity === null) {\n    appendNotice('No key material loaded');\n    return;\n  }\n\n  try {\n    // An existing session is reused whichever role this party holds in it. A\n    // handshake is run only when none exists, since each one consumes a\n    // one-time prekey from the recipient's finite published batch.\n    const session = existingSession(to) ?? (await sessionAsInitiator(identity, to));\n    announce(session);\n\n    const { payload, header } = encryptMessage(session, text, identity.username, to);\n    send({ type: 'chat', to, text: payload, header });\n  } catch (error) {\n    // Reached when the recipient has published no key material, and also when\n    // the policy requires the hybrid agreement and they cannot provide it.\n    console.error('Could not send message:', error);\n    appendNotice(`Could not send to ${to}: ${String(error)}`);\n    return;\n  }\n\n  appendChat('you', text);\n  messageInput.value = '';\n  messageInput.focus();\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Wiring\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nconnectButton.addEventListener('click', () => void connect());\nsendButton.addEventListener('click', () => void sendMessage());\n\nusernameInput.addEventListener('keydown', (event: KeyboardEvent) => {\n  if (event.key === 'Enter') {\n    void connect();\n  }\n});\n\nmessageInput.addEventListener('keydown', (event: KeyboardEvent) => {\n  if (event.key === 'Enter') {\n    void sendMessage();\n  }\n});\n\n/**\n * Changing the policy discards every established session.\n *\n * A session already derived under one agreement is not retroactively covered\n * by another, so leaving it in place would leave the interface reporting a\n * protection that the conversation does not have. Discarding forces a fresh\n * handshake under the new policy, which costs one of the correspondent's\n * one-time prekeys and is the honest outcome.\n */\nmodeSelect.addEventListener('change', () => {\n  const policy = modeSelect.value as ModePolicy;\n  setModePolicy(policy);\n  clearSessions();\n  announced.clear();\n  appendNotice(`Key agreement policy set to \"${policy}\"; existing sessions discarded`);\n});\n\n/**\n * Development aid: discards local key material and every session derived from\n * it, then closes the connection.\n *\n * Closing matters. Leaving the socket open would keep this client operating\n * with keys held only in memory, which no longer exist in storage and whose\n * public halves the registry may have replaced, producing failures that look\n * like protocol defects rather than a deliberate reset.\n */\nresetButton.addEventListener('click', () => {\n  const username = usernameInput.value.trim();\n  if (!USERNAME_PATTERN.test(username)) {\n    window.alert('Enter the username whose keys should be discarded.');\n    return;\n  }\n  void clearIdentity(username).then(() => {\n    clearSessions();\n    announced.clear();\n    identity = null;\n    socket?.close();\n    window.alert(`Discarded local keys for ${username}. Reload before reconnecting.`);\n  });\n});\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Start-up\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * Confirms that both primitives work in this browser before any conversation\n * depends on them.\n *\n * Each check performs a complete exchange rather than merely generating a key,\n * because initialising successfully and agreeing on a secret are different\n * claims, and only the second is the one the handshake rests on.\n */\nasync function verifyCryptoAvailable(): Promise<void> {\n  // The application's entry point is where the concrete implementation is\n  // chosen; the module that uses it depends only on the capability.\n  useMlKemFactory(createMLKEM768);\n\n  try {\n    await initSodium();\n    const shared = await verifyX25519Exchange();\n    console.log('libsodium ready; X25519 exchange agrees. Shared secret:', toBase64(shared));\n\n    const mlkem = await verifyMlKemExchange();\n    console.log(\n      `ML-KEM-768 ready; encapsulation agrees in ${mlkem.elapsedMs.toFixed(2)} ms.`,\n      `Shared secret: ${toBase64(mlkem.sharedSecret)}`\n    );\n  } catch (error) {\n    console.error('Cryptographic bootstrap failed:', error);\n    appendNotice('Cryptography unavailable in this browser');\n  }\n}\n\nvoid verifyCryptoAvailable();\n"],
